Economic overview 
Kazakhstan continues to benefit from one of the strongest sovereign and external 
positions in the CCA region, supported by sizeable sovereign assets, a balanced 
network of international partners and higher oil prices. Strong portfolio inflows, 
attractive real rates, progress towards Euroclear connectivity, growing domestic 
debt supply and positive rating momentum have supported the tenge and local 
markets, while a sizeable backlog of future state FX sales provides an additional 
buffer against market stress. 
However, these strengths coexist with persistent current-account deficits, continued 
fiscal and quasi-fiscal stimulus, limited private sector investment and an uneven 
track record in fiscal discipline and inflation targeting. Greater reliance on portfolio 
inflows is also making the tenge more sensitive to changes in global risk appetite. 
This creates a growing trade-off between the benefits of deeper foreign 
participation in the local market and greater sensitivity to shifts in global 
positioning. Current inflation trends leave room for moderate monetary easing, but 
domestic fiscal and quasi-fiscal stimulus alongside external price risks should keep 
the easing cycle gradual. 
Economic activity: growth resilient despite oil disruptions and VAT hike 
GDP growth has slowed this year compared to the exceptional pace seen in 2025 but 
remains robust despite the VAT hike and oil-related disruptions. Disruptions to oil 
production and exports appear to have been the main drag on growth in 1H26 and 
remain the main risk for 2H26.  
Non-oil sectors continue to perform relatively well, while higher oil prices support 
confidence and improve the fiscal and external outlook. Domestic demand also appears 
supported by quasi-fiscal stimulus estimated at 4-5% of GDP. At the same time, retail 
and corporate lending are showing signs of cooling amid tight monetary conditions. We 
see GDP growth remaining in the 4.5-5.0% range in the coming years. 
Fig 30 Kazakhstan: GDP growth dynamics and structure  Fig 31 Kazakhstan: Key oil sector indicators

Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

Budget policy: Consolidation ongoing 
Headline fiscal policy has entered a consolidation phase, reflected in lower reliance on 
National Fund (NFRK) transfers to finance current spending, although a durable track 
record of fiscal discipline has yet to be established.

In addition to the quasi-fiscal stimulus provided through Baiterek lending, during 2026, 
the three-year state spending plan increased by KZT6.5tr to be carried out through 
targeted transfers from the National Fund (NFRK). This fits the previous pattern of 
spending plans being revised upwards over time. At the same time, even with the 
additional spending, the overall volume of National Fund spending, including guaranteed 
and targeted transfers, is still expected to decline, in our estimates, to 1.5-2.5% of GDP 
over the medium term.  
Importantly, however, this should have little effect on the headline republican (central 
government) budget deficit, which should remain in the 2-3% GDP range, determining 
the annual net debt issuance requirement. The consolidated budget should gravitate 
around balance but can also be subject to volatility due to the large role of NFRK’s 
investment income, which reached 4% of GDP in 2025. 
Fig 32 Kazakhstan: Republican budget balance  Fig 33 Kazakhstan: Consolidated budget balance

Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

Inflation and monetary policy: Real rate to remain high 
The moderate easing in inflation from its 2025 peak and a resilient tenge have allowed 
the NBK to start cutting the base rate in 2026. Inflation pressures from the VAT hike and 
global commodity-price spike have remained under control for now, while portfolio 
inflows and attractive real yields have given an additional boost to the currency. At the 
same time, tariff increases, fiscal and quasi-fiscal stimulus, and producer-price pressures 
amid global inflation risks continue to justify our above-consensus CPI outlook.  
Therefore, we expect the easing cycle to continue only gradually, while remaining alert 
to potential external inflation shocks. Although Kazakhstan retains room for nominal 
easing, real policy rates are likely to remain high by regional standards and supportive 
of capital inflows.

Balance of payments and exchange rate: Portfolio flows increasingly matter 
Higher oil prices have strengthened Kazakhstan's external position, although part of the 
benefit is offset by weaker export volumes. Despite some recent improvement, the 
country continues to run structural current account deficits as strong imports and 
dividend outflows offset export revenues.  
Fig 36 Kazakhstan: Fuel export volume and value  Fig 37 Kazakhstan: Balance of payment composition

Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

As a result, capital flows are becoming increasingly important for KZT dynamics. 
Progress towards Euroclear connectivity, positive rating momentum and additional 
public and corporate debt issuance should continue attracting portfolio inflows amid 
high real rates, supporting the tenge. Kazakhstan's capital market modernisation is also 
broadening beyond international settlement access, with the NBK now experimenting 
with tokenised debt issuance alongside the expanding digital tenge infrastructure. We 
have improved our KZT outlook but note that greater reliance on portfolio capital in an 
economy with persistent twin deficits also increases sensitivity to shifts in global risk 
appetite and flow reversals.  
The good news, however, is that the government has recently accumulated a backlog of 
FX sales linked to National Fund spending, which we estimate at c.US$2bn for June-
October. An unwinding of that backlog could provide an additional buffer for the 
domestic FX market in the event of external stress. 
Fig 38 Kazakhstan: Non-resident investments and KZT  Fig 39 Kazakhstan: NBRK/NFRK FX sales and KZT

Sovereign credit strategy 
Overall, the underlying fundamental momentum is generally positive, with fiscal 
consolidation under close focus, while higher energy prices should offer a modest boost 
to export and budget revenues. However, as with budget policy, on the issuance front, 
more quasi-sovereign supply remains a risk in the coming years given low spread levels 
compared to most BBB peers.

Economic overview  
Kyrgyzstan is the newest addition to our CCA coverage and arguably the region’s 
most striking growth story. Economic growth remains exceptionally strong, 
supported by construction and trade activity, infrastructure investment, remittance 
inflows and rapid credit growth. Public finances remain relatively comfortable and 
international reserves have risen sharply, while the stability of the som has helped 
insulate the economy from external price pressures. 
However, the current pace of growth also points to significant overheating risks. 
Inflation is already well above the central bank’s target range, credit expansion 
remains rapid and real rates offer little evidence of tight monetary conditions, while 
the recent slowdown in corporate deposit inflows points to greater volatility in the 
corporate funding base. Large external deficits, dependence on Russia-related 
remittances and unusually opaque external flows further complicate the picture. 
The recent sovereign rating improvement is encouraging, but Kyrgyzstan remains 
an early-stage catch-up story in which macro credibility is still lagging the headline 
growth. 
Economic activity: Exceptional growth, increasingly demand-led 
Despite recent moderation to 9.7% YoY as of 3Q26, economic growth in Kyrgyzstan 
remains exceptionally strong at 12.3% YoY on a rolling four-quarter basis, with trade and 
construction alone contributing around 9.6 percentage points. Construction has become 
a significantly more important growth engine since 2022, supported by infrastructure 
investment, while strong trade activity continues to reflect both domestic consumption 
and Kyrgyzstan's role in regional transit trade. Industrial performance, by contrast, 
remains more volatile. 
The composition of growth makes the current double-digit pace unlikely to be sustained 
indefinitely. Some of the exceptional post-2022 boost from regional trade rerouting 
should gradually fade, while activity is increasingly reliant on domestic investment and 
consumption. Large infrastructure projects should keep growth relatively strong, but the 
challenge is to convert the recent acceleration into broader productivity gains rather 
than a prolonged demand boom.

Fiscal policy: Headline surpluses mask a growing investment push 
Kyrgyzstan’s consolidated budget remains comfortably in surplus by 2.5-3.0% of GDP, 
supported by rapid economic growth and strong revenues, but the headline balance 
increasingly understates the expansionary direction of fiscal policy. Direct expenditures 
have been growing faster than GDP-driven tax revenues, largely reflecting the state’s 
infrastructure and investment push, as spending on ‘economic policies’ in the fiscal 
statistics is currently almost 5 percentage points of GDP higher than two years ago, 
eroding the underlying balance of tax revenues and direct expenditures. More 
importantly, once volatile non-tax revenues associated with financial and capital 
transactions are excluded, the fiscal position moves into a widening deficit. This helps 
explain why government borrowing has continued to increase despite the headline 
surplus. 
Nominal public debt has more than doubled from KGS436bn in 2021 to around 
KGS900bn currently, even as rapid nominal GDP growth has brought the debt ratio down 
sharply and more recently kept it broadly stable at around 40% of GDP. Recent 
borrowing, including the recent US$700m Eurobond placement in 2025, reflects 
primarily government financial transactions, including operations involving capital 
injections into state-owned enterprises, as suggested by the recent IMF analysis. The 
growing role of such operations means that the composition and use of new borrowing 
are becoming increasingly important for assessing Kyrgyzstan’s underlying fiscal 
position and sovereign credit story. 
Part of this borrowing reflects an infrastructure-heavy growth model, including the CKU 
railway and Kambarata-1. This raises potential growth if successfully executed, but also 
makes project execution, SOE balance sheets and the structure of external funding 
increasingly important for sovereign investors. 
Fig 42 Kyrgyzstan: Consolidated budget balance   Fig 43 Kyrgyzstan: Key public debt metrics

Source: National sources, IMF, CEIC, ING  Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

Inflation and monetary policy: Overheating sends hawkish signals 
Inflation has accelerated well beyond the National Bank's 5-7% target range. Domestic 
tariff increases were an important contributor in the first half of 2026, but price 
pressures have subsequently broadened, with strong demand adding to external food 
and other cost pressures. The relative stability of the som has provided an important 
offset to imported inflation. 
This leaves monetary policy in a difficult position. With inflation broadly matching the 
12% policy rate, monetary conditions do not look particularly tight. Rapid money and 
credit growth and abundant liquidity also point to weak transmission. We expect further 
near-term tightening, followed by scope for partial reversal during 2027 if inflation 
moderates. We also think stronger liquidity management, alongside the policy rate, will 
be important if the National Bank is to bring inflation sustainably back into its target 
range.

Balance of payments and KGS: Strong buffers, vulnerable external accounts 
Som stability masks a much more complicated external position. Kyrgyzstan runs a 
structurally large merchandise trade deficit, currently at US$11bn on a 12-month rolling 
basis, increasingly driven by machinery and other imports from China and other non-CIS 
economies. Some of these imports reflect the domestic investment boom, while part of 
the exceptional trade expansion has been related to regional re-exports. The latter 
appears to have peaked in 2024-2025 and has normalised since then. Meanwhile, trade 
ties with Russia remain deep, with bilateral trade exceeding US$4bn per annum, supporting 
near-term activity but also reinforcing Kyrgyzstan's exposure to Russia-related external risks. 
Remittance inflows provide an important counterweight to the trade deficit. Gross 
inflows remain close to record levels of US$3bn per annum and continue to be 
dominated by Russia (90%), while net remittances of around 11% of GDP provide 
substantial support to household incomes, domestic demand and the FX market. This 
provides a large buffer but leaves Kyrgyzstan heavily exposed to economic and policy 
developments in Russia. 
Fig 46 Kyrgyzstan: Trade balance by region  Fig 47 Kyrgyzstan: Net remittances

Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

Official BOP data requires particular caution; IMF estimates suggest that unrecorded  
re-exports materially distort the measured current account deficit. Structural current 
account deficits of US$5-7bn per annum require continued external financing, while 
large unrecorded trade and financial flows make the headline current account numbers 
unusually difficult to interpret. FDI and, more recently, portfolio flows have helped 
finance the external funding gap. International reserves have risen sharply in nominal 
terms, but much of the increase reflects gold revaluation; physical gold holdings have 
remained broadly stable. 
For now, these buffers and the managed exchange rate regime should continue 
ensuring som stability, seen since mid-2025. But the combination of a large trade deficit,

Banking sector: Rapid financial deepening brings its own risks 
Loan growth in Kyrgyzstan remains very strong, particularly in retail, which is expanding 
rapidly from a relatively low base, though the pace has recently moderated. Corporate 
loan growth is accelerating and catching up with retail, with FX lending playing a stable 
role. Meanwhile, deposit and broader funding growth has slowed. Most notably, 
corporate deposit inflows slowed materially, and this stands out compared to other 
segments of the banking sector. 
The initial drop appears partly related to large corporate and government payments in the 
middle of the year, but the subsequent weakness may also reflect tighter sanctions 
compliance, including on the A7 payment network and the ruble-linked A757 
cryptocurrency, as well as new restrictions and fees affecting non-resident corporate 
accounts.   
On the lending side, the low starting point means that part of the expansion represents 
welcome financial deepening. However, rapid credit creation is also reinforcing domestic 
demand and weakening monetary policy transmission at a time when inflation is 
already high. Credit quality, bank liquidity and the pace at which lending continues to 
outgrow the funding base are therefore becoming increasingly important macro-
financial indicators to watch. 
Fig 50 Kyrgyzstan: Bank funding indicators  Fig 51 Kyrgyzstan: Bank lending indicators

Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

Sovereign credit strategy 
While Kyrgyzstan remains an interesting reform story for EM investors, recent 
performance has seen spreads squeeze tight versus peers. Headline fiscal metrics look 
strong but mask increased spending and borrowing needs, which could drive further

Economic overview 
Uzbekistan remains our preferred medium-term CCA growth and reform story, but 
strong recent performance also brings increasing signs of overheating and leaves 
important macro imbalances unresolved. Growth remains well above potential, 
inflation is still above target and persistent twin deficits coexist with continued high 
state involvement in the economy and a much-reduced liquid component of the 
sovereign wealth fund. At the same time, reform and privatisation momentum, high 
real rates and strong capital inflows have supported the soum, while unusually 
restrained physical gold exports leave potential for additional FX supply if sales 
normalise. 
We therefore retain a stronger-than-consensus medium-term view on UZS and see 
scope for gradual monetary easing, but a durable break from its historical 
depreciation trend requires further disinflation, fiscal discipline and evidence that 
private capital inflows can remain strong as the current reform and privatisation 
push matures. 
Economic activity: Accelerating beyond potential 
Uzbekistan’s GDP growth has been exceptionally strong recently, supported by domestic 
demand catching up from a low base. While activity is expected to moderate from the 
current above-potential levels, Uzbekistan should remain one of the fastest-growing 
economies in emerging markets. Strong investment spending, industrial activity and 
ongoing reforms continue to support expansion, while positive demographic trends 
provide an additional tailwind.  
A gradual slowdown in the GDP growth is likely to reflect normalisation in the activity 
trends, with growth remaining comfortably above regional peers. The immediate 
challenge is managing overheating as consumption and investment continue to grow 
rapidly.  
Fig 53 Uzbekistan: GDP growth composition  Fig 54 Uzbekistan: Key activity indicators

Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

Fiscal policy: Pace of consolidation moderated 
Fiscal consolidation is continuing, with the general government deficit narrowing to 1.1% 
of GDP in the four quarters to 3Q26, from 4.1% in 2023. The pace of improvement has 
slowed recently, partly because unusually low gold exports have constrained revenues, 
which we expect to be temporary. At the same time, expenditure growth is catching up 
with revenue growth, pointing to a more structural challenge for Uzbekistan’s fiscal policy.

The government also has strong incentives to maintain fiscal restraint. Domestic 
demand is already running hot, while several years of budget deficits have reduced the 
UFRD's liquid component from 17% of GDP in 2019 to around 3% in 2025. We see fiscal 
deficits limited to around 1% of GDP on the three-year horizon, with a growing share of 
debt financing and privatisation as a source of financing, which should be taken 
positively by portfolio investors. 
The privatisation programme, though stemming largely from the fiscal needs, is gaining 
importance as a capital-markets story. Following the UzNIF transaction, the widening 
pipeline of state assets creates scope for international capital raising.

Inflation and monetary policy: Scope for gradual easing 
Over the past couple of years, inflation in Uzbekistan has moderated substantially from 
previous highs, aided by the recent fiscal consolidation, and this has created room for a 
cautious monetary policy easing cycle. However, current CPI remains above the central 
bank's long-term objective, while strong domestic demand, administrative tariff 
adjustments and global food and commodity risks argue against aggressive rate cuts.  
We therefore expect the central bank to deliver only gradual easing from a still-
restrictive level. Real rates remain relatively attractive and should continue to support 
macro stability while allowing policymakers to balance growth objectives against the 
need for further disinflation.

Our stronger-than-consensus medium-term view on the soum continues to be 
reinforced by recent developments. High real rates, gold prices, an expected catch-up in 
physical gold exports and growing foreign participation in Uzbekistan's privatisation 
programme provide important support for the balance of payments and FX market. The 
investment pipeline continues to broaden geographically, with a recently announced

CCA macro and credit November 2026

28 
package of US-linked projects potentially worth US$12bn upon implementation, spanning 
transport, critical minerals, infrastructure and financial services. This should underpin the 
resilience of the UZS despite the country's persistent trade and current-account deficits.  
Uzbekistan’s physical gold exports have been significantly restrained this year, at just 18 
tonnes in January-September 2026 compared with 85 tonnes in the same period of 
2025. This means that the benefit from the gold price rally has so far been limited on the 
external trade side. The slight appreciation of the soum against major currencies this 
year appears to have been driven largely by capital inflows, partly supported by high 
real rates, as has been the case in Kazakhstan. 
Fig 59 Uzbekistan: CBRU monthly gold exports  Fig 60 Uzbekistan: Balance of payments

Source: National sources, CEIC, ING  Source: National sources, CEIC, ING

The reason for the unusually low sales has not been formally explained. However, the 
simultaneous increase in the central bank's physical gold holdings – to 453.5 tonnes in 
October - indicates deliberate retention amid continued extraction. The significant 
restraint on gold exports so far this year suggests some upside potential for the soum if 
export volumes recover towards their historical range of 80-120 tonnes per year. This 
catch-up in delayed gold exports is one of the factors we see supporting the currency 
over the medium term. 
However, the longer-term outlook is less certain, as Uzbekistan continues to run sizeable 
twin deficits, while inflation remains above target. As a result, we view the UZS positively 
in the medium-term but believe a durable break from the historical depreciation trend 
would require sustained progress in reducing macro imbalances and deepening 
structural reforms. 
Sovereign credit strategy 
The fundamental picture for Uzbekistan has improved significantly in recent years, with 
narrowing twin deficits amid solid growth reflected in rating upgrades. Fitch’s positive 
outlook suggests there is still scope for improvement to continue, while the stronger 
credit profile should increase the country’s resilience to the current energy shock. An 
expected catch-up in gold exports, alongside ongoing capital inflows linked to the 
privatisation pipeline should keep the momentum strong, and we see the potential for 
more fundamental upside.

## Document 353: How Polish households are saving and investing in 2026

Source PDF: all report pdf/How Polish households are saving and investing in 2026.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Why Poland’s new Personal Investment Account could prove
popular with Polish investors
Leszek Kasek
Poles remain cautious investors, with a preference for cash and bank deposits
Our recent survey paints a positive picture of Polish households’
ﬁnances. A large share have savings, many are open to investing in
capital markets for the ﬁrst time, and welcome regulatory and tax
incentives.
Against this backdrop, the new Personal Investment Account
scheme (OKI), due to come into force in 2027, could ﬁnd fertile

ground. It addresses the knowledge gap, encourages regular saving
and investing, and oﬀers tax relief. The scheme could help Poles
shift from conservative savers towards more informed investors.
An IPSOS survey commissioned by ING Bank Śląski on Poles’
attitudes towards saving and investing (a sample of 1,001
respondents), compared with selected European countries
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.

You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies
Save preferences Accept all

### Page 2

(including Germany, Spain, the Netherlands and Romania), found
that Poles:
View their ﬁnancial situation more optimistically, although
they are not among the wealthiest in Europe.
Are more likely to report having savings, although these are
relatively lower than in other countries.
Prefer liquidity (cash and bank deposits) to the potential returns
oﬀered by other ﬁnancial instruments, indicating greater risk
aversion.
These conservative attitudes are borne out by ‘hard’ National

Bank of Poland data on Polish households’ ﬁnancial assets.
Are also more likely than other Europeans to describe investing
in ﬁnancial instruments as being as risky as gambling in a
casino.
Prefer to invest in their own country.
Are more likely to regard property and gold as good investment
alternatives.
Often associate saving with concerns about retirement.
Like other Europeans, they say they lack knowledge about
investing and view schemes supporting saving and investment

through government regulation and tax incentives more
favourably.
This could provide a promising environment for the Open
Investment Account scheme (OKI) in 2027. By combining tax
incentives with measures to improve ﬁnancial knowledge and
encourage regular investing, the scheme could help shift Polish
households away from a traditionally conservative approach
towards more active and informed investment.
About the survey: The survey of individuals’ saving and investment

preferences covered 11 European countries (Belgium, France, Spain,
the Netherlands, Germany, Poland, Romania, Switzerland, Türkiye,
the United Kingdom and Italy) and Australia. Samples comprised
around 1,000 people in each country and were representative in
terms of sex, age, marital status, education, labour market activity,
household size and monthly income.

### Page 3

Key ﬁndings from comparing Poland with
other European countries
In this article, we compare the survey results for Poland with the
average for European countries (Europe), as well as the results for
the Netherlands and Germany (countries wealthier than Poland),
Spain (a country similar to Poland in terms of population and GDP
per capita) and Romania (a country with lower GDP per capita than
Poland).
Poles feel better off, despite lagging
European wealth levels

75% of Polish respondents believe they are living comfortably
or doing quite well, compared with a European average of 55%
and just under 50% in Romania.
Poles assess their ﬁnancial situation optimistically, although in
2025 their stock of net ﬁnancial assets (assets minus liabilities)
relative to GDP (78% of GDP) was less than half the EU27
average (165% of GDP). Compared with 2017, net assets
increased by around 13% of GDP, but this was driven by a

decline in liabilities (deleveraging), while the stock of ﬁnancial
assets remained stable (at around 100% of GDP).
According to Eurostat data on the composition of gross
ﬁnancial assets, around half of Poles’ assets (49%, compared
with 30% in the EU27) consisted of cash and bank deposits
(current and term deposits) in 2025. The share of cash
increased markedly after the pandemic (from 9% of ﬁnancial
assets in 2017 to 11% in 2025) and was the highest among

the countries under review. Compared with wealthier European
countries, Poland stood out for the signiﬁcant share of loans
(5% of the total), most of which comprised retail Treasury
savings bonds. In EU statistics, these instruments are treated as
loans to the public sector rather than debt securities because of
their limited transferability (only bonds listed on the Warsaw
Stock Exchange are included in the latter category). Compared
with the EU27, Poland has a lower share of shares and other

equity in total ﬁnancial assets (19% versus 27%), as well as
investment fund shares or units (6% versus 11%), and
particularly a lower share of assets in insurance and pension
schemes (14% versus 26%).

### Page 6

Compared with other countries, relatively many Poles tend to
agree that their household has too little savings to invest.
Poles favour liquidity over higher returns,
indicating greater risk aversion
Source: IPSOS survey
Around 40% of Poles say they prefer to keep their savings liquid
so that they can spend them easily if needed. This attitude
does not diﬀer substantially from the European average; the
proportions in Germany and the Netherlands are actually

signiﬁcantly higher.
Almost 70% say they do not invest in capital market
instruments such as shares, bonds, investment funds or ETFs.
This proportion is slightly higher than the European average,
but lower than in the Netherlands. Among those who said they
did not currently invest, however, Poland has the highest
proportion of respondents open to taking this step in future
(29%) (I have not invested, but I may do so one day), compared
with 24% in Europe. This indicates Poles’ openness to new

forms of investing.
Poles’ views on long-term returns reﬂect
investment caution
More than half of Poles (55%) regard gold and property as the
most proﬁtable investments, compared with a European
average of 46%. (These categories are not classiﬁed as
ﬁnancial assets in Eurostat statistics).

## Document 405: The Commodities Feed_ Metals under pressure while oil supply improves

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Commodities Feed_ Metals under pressure while oil supply improves.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Oil remains under pressure as Middle East supply recovers, while
aluminium tightness keeps nearby spreads elevated. Copper
and iron ore continue to face pressure from a softer demand
backdrop

Ewa Manthey and Warren Patterson
Brent is trading below $92/bbl as improving supply conditions outweigh
continued geopolitical risks in the Middle East
Energy - Oil extends decline as supply
concerns ease
Oil prices extended their decline for a second session, with Brent
trading below $92/bbl as improving supply conditions outweighed
continued geopolitical risks in the Middle East. The physical picture
has improved considerably in recent months. Crude oil and fuel
ﬂows through the Strait of Hormuz rose to 9.1mb/d in 3Q26 from
5.1mb/d in the previous quarter. However, ﬂows remain well below
the 21.4mb/d recorded a year earlier, highlighting that regional
supply has yet to fully normalise, as data from the Energy
Information Administration (EIA) shows.
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies
Save preferences Accept all

### Page 2

Other indicators also point to easing near-term tightness. Brent’s
prompt spread has narrowed to around $2.40/bbl in backwardation
from more than $3/bbl a month ago. Meanwhile, speculative
positioning has continued to weaken, with money managers
cutting their net long in NYMEX WTI by 14,719 lots to 70,584 lots
last week, marking a seventh consecutive weekly decline.
Geopolitical risks remain elevated, with continued attacks on
vessels highlighting the vulnerability of regional export

infrastructure. However, improving ﬂows from the Persian Gulf and
expectations for a looser global oil balance are increasingly
oﬀsetting these concerns. We expect improving supply availability
to remain a headwind for oil prices, although any renewed
disruption to ﬂows through the Strait of Hormuz remains the key
upside risk.
Elsewhere, US natural gas prices extended their decline as
improving supply and forecasts for warmer weather weighed on
the market. Production in the Appalachian region appears to be

recovering as producers bring previously withheld volumes back to
the market, while above-average temperatures forecast for parts of
the Midwest and Northeast should reduce near-term heating
demand. However, strong LNG demand should provide some
support, with ﬂows to US export terminals reaching record levels
and limiting the amount of gas available to the domestic market.
Metals - Gold rebounds from recent lows
Gold edged higher this morning after falling to its lowest close since

September 2025 in the previous session. The modest rebound
comes after a sharp sell-oﬀ driven by persistent US inﬂation, higher
Treasury yields and growing expectations that the Federal Reserve
will keep monetary policy tighter for longer. Weakness in
technology stocks has also weighed on bullion recently as investors
reduced exposure to cover losses elsewhere. Still, the prospect of
further Fed tightening remains a headwind for precious metals.
Attention now turns to the minutes from the Fed’s October meeting

for further clues on the outlook for interest rates. We expect gold to
Your privacy is important

Targeting Cookies

### Page 3

remain volatile in the near term, with the direction of US yields and
the dollar likely to remain the key drivers.
In base metals, copper is trading around its lowest level in more
than four months as weakness in technology stocks and softer
Chinese economic data has weighed on sentiment. The sell-oﬀ in
AI-related shares has raised concerns around the demand outlook
for copper, given the metal’s growing exposure to investment in
power grids and data centres.

Weaker Chinese industrial and production data yesterday added to
concerns over demand from the world’s largest copper consumer.
However, signs of tightness remain in China’s domestic market,
where near-term contracts are trading at a sizeable premium to
later-dated prices amid depleted inventories and constrained
availability.
Meanwhile, aluminium’s nearby market has tightened sharply, with
the Tom/next spread rising above $115/t and spot metal
commanding a premium of more than $250/t over the three-

month contract. Low exchange inventories have left the market
particularly sensitive to demand for prompt metal, pushing the
front of the LME curve deeper into backwardation. We expect this
tightness to continue supporting prices in the near term. Further
out, however, the supply picture looks less supportive as stronger
production growth should gradually improve availability and
loosen the global balance next year.
Elsewhere, iron ore extended its decline, with Chinese futures falling

to their lowest level in four years at the start of the week as
concerns over the demand outlook intensiﬁed. Sentiment remains
weak amid falling Chinese steel production, poor mill margins and
elevated iron ore inventories at ports. The supply outlook is also
adding pressure, with expectations of strong shipments through the
end of the year and the gradual ramp-up of the Simandou mine in
Guinea. China’s crude steel output fell further in October to its

lowest level since early 2025, reinforcing concerns over demand for
steelmaking raw materials. While seasonal restocking ahead of

不同於台灣投資⼈沒有  non-GAAP 或調整後財報數字作為參考，美國會計準
則及分析師通常將併購後的 5-6 年商譽攤銷，⾮經常性費⽤排除在調整後⽑利
率，營業利潤率，及 EPS 之外，然後來分析其獲利變化。歸因於新興雲公司，
SoW AI 公司  Cerebras, 新興測試設備公司  Aehr 及 Cohu ，光纖通訊 InP 基
板公司  AXT 於 2026 年上半年仍多處於虧損狀態， Adj EPS 年增⽐較數字就變

前 5 名明顯跟 AI 運算及光纖互聯晶⽚相關。

Superior rule of 40

### Page 6

歸因於我們精選的 20 家美國上市的科技及半導體公司都是營收⾼增⻑公司，加
上⼤多數公司都有雙位數的營業利潤率（除了⾕歌主要的系統組裝公司
Celestica 只有 7% 營業利潤率外），所以其  rule of 40 指標都遠遠⾼於產業
平均， 2027 年超過 100% 的有 12 家，即使 2028 年營收年增減速，超過 100%
的仍有 5 家。
Source: Andrew Lu on global semis/techs
Valuation matters
美國精選 20 中有 7 家（ Cerebras System, Applied Optoelectronics,
SpaceXSI, Cohu, AXT, FTAI Aviation, AMD) 有機會在 2027 年的  adj EPS
倍增，⽽前 3 家  adj EPS 2026-2030 年複合增⻑率皆超過 100% （ Applied
Optoelectronics adj EPS 預測僅到 2028 ），尤其在 10-11 ⽉份科技及半導體

公司股價⼤幅拉回之後，美國精選 20 的⼤部分公司在短期來看都已回到低於
30x 2027 adj EPS 的  P/E 估值 , 雖然  Cerebras ， SpaceXSI ， Palantir ，
Bloom Energy ，  Aehr 都還交易在 30x 以上的  2027 adj EPS ，但如果⽤
2027-2030 年 4 年平均  P/E 來跟 2026-2030 年 4 年  adj EPS 複合增⻑率來⽐
較，這些公司的 4 年平均  PEG 都還是維持在 1.0 以下，這其中較為偏⾼的也只
有  Palantir 的 0.82 PEG 及 Aehr 的 0.99 PEG ⽐較接近 1 。
我們從最近美國精選 20 的股價震盪回檔來看，雖然短中期基本⾯仍然紮實，
但⻑期投資⼈已經開始考量以下 4 ⼤⾵險是否將影響⻑期營收 /adj EPS 獲利
預測及 PEG 估值被下修的⾵險。  1. Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang 離職對
Nvidia 及 AI 產業技術疊代群⿓無⾸的⻑期影響； 2. 美國⺠主黨參眾議院及州

⻑選舉⼤勝是否將嚴重放緩 AI 基礎建設，還是也想分⼀杯羹； 3. AI 數據算⼒
中⼼的基礎建設是否將被缺電，缺電網，缺資⾦⽽減緩？ 4. 很多轉租算⼒給
中國⼤陸客戶的主權 AI 市場是否被美國商務部擴⼤管制？

## Document 436: When tokenization meets securitized debt markets (2026-11-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/When tokenization meets securitized debt markets (2026-11-17).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank Research Institute
Deutsche Bank
Research

### Page 2

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
When tokenization meets securitized debt markets
Asset tokenization continues to grow in 2026. Excluding stablecoins, the
market grew from ~$10bn in Jan 2025 to ~$40bn in Nov 2026. Including
tokens confined to closed or permissioned environments, the total reached
~$420bn (here).
This chartbook explores tokenization’s potential in securitized markets, where

complex structuring, long settlement times and high transaction costs make
the asset class a strong candidate for tokenization.
Today, tokenization mainly improves securities-market infrastructure by
streamlining intermediation, cutting loan origination costs and speeding up
institutional securitization. US-based companies Figure Technologies,
Centrifuge and Maple Finance illustrate the benefits.
The transformative end state combines tokenized infrastructure (represented

tokenization) with tokens transferable outside the issuing platform (distributed
tokenization). Only when both are combined could tokenization reduce credit
risk, deepen the investor base, speed up the flow of capital, and lead to a more
robust, liquid US securitized market.
In this chartbook, we: (i) explain securitization and its challenges; (ii) examine
tokenization’s potential benefits; and (iii) outline the steps needed for
widespread adoption.

….but only ~$8bn in distributed value.
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, RWA.xyz. Data as of Nov 12, 2026. Credit combines asset-backed credit,
corporate credit, diversified credit and specialty finance. “Represented” refers to tokens using the blockchain
as a recordkeeping layer, enhancing transparency, reconciliation, and operational efficiency without enabling
onchain investor transfer or distribution.
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, RWA.xyz. Data as of Nov 13, 2026. “Distributed”

refers to tokens transferable outside the issuing platform and between wallets; it does
not measure trading activity.
Tokenized credit’s total value is ~$50bn in November, with Figure’s HELOC
token representing $26trn…

### Page 3

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
A ~$15trn market financing housing, consumers and companies
❑ The securitized (structured credit) market includes residential real estate
(RMBS), commercial real estate (CMBS), consumer borrowing (ABS –
autos, credit cards, student loans), and corporate debt (CLOs).
❑ It is a material part of the US growth engine: ~$15trn of debt outstanding,
roughly half of 2025 US nominal GDP. About 75% is linked to US housing

market.
❑ This market emerged in the late 1970s, when the US economy struggled
with sluggish growth and high inflation from elevated energy costs.
❑ Securitization was seen as financial innovation: It let bank lenders move
debt off their balance sheets and syndicate it to public markets as a
pooled asset.
❑ In the three decades before the GFC, the market’s growth increased
capital velocity in US debt markets while simultaneously reducing
financing costs for both borrowers and originators.

❑ Securitization produces pricing efficiency by bundling a diversified pool
of assets with consistent cashflows (e.g., such as corporate loans or
monthly mortgage payments) into a security held by a bankruptcy remote
special purpose vehicle (SPV).
❑ These pooled assets are then sliced into “tranches” with varying credit
risk and corresponding levels of yield, sold to institutional investors (life
insurers to hedge funds) that find relative value in the structure and

diversification.
Residential mortgages (RMBS) plays an outsized role in the market
The US Securitized market is approaching $16trn in market size
Sources: SIFMA, Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank Research.
Sources: SIFMA, US Federal Reserve, Deutsche Bank Research.

### Page 4

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
Securitization is held back by complexity, slow settlement and high costs
A CLO investor can expect their first coupon payment typically 6 months
after a deal’s pricing date
Although securitization is deeply embedded into the US financial
system, several challenges remain:
❑ Inherent complexity: Collateralization complicates ownership
tracking, especially as deals are upsized, widely distributed and

traded in a secondary market. This was visible during the GFC, and in
recent pockets of credit stress – the tracking of collateral and of
asset ownership can present logistical hurdles over the lifetime of an
asset.
❑ Long settlement times: Complex structuring can delay settlement
and prolong the liquidity gap between allocating capital to a deal and
when the first coupon distribution takes place.  For example, a new
issue CLO can take up to two months from pricing to close, with an

additional four months before the first coupon distribution date takes
place.
❑ High transaction costs: Intermediaries between the buyer and seller
provide essential services but increase costs when issuing a deal.
Additionally, the ongoing deal administration from monthly collateral
monitoring to coupon distributions adds an additional layer of
logistical complexity to a securitization post close. The key
stakeholders required in this value chain range from arranging banks,

trustees, rating agencies, and lawyers.
Source: Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank Research.
4

### Page 5

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
❑ Tokenization records assets or claims as digital tokens on a distributed
ledger (i.e., blockchain), using public or permissioned networks.
❑ Potential benefits: Faster transfers, automated servicing and smaller
investment units.
❑ Shared records can reduce reconciliation, but trading hours, redemption
terms and investor access remain product-specific.
Two issuance models:

❑ On-chain (native): The security or fund share is issued directly on the
distributed ledger/blockchain.
❑ Off-chain (tokenized representation): The underlying asset remains
outside the blockchain, but ownership claims and economic rights are
represented digitally on-chain.
❑ Tokens may be then either transferred to external wallets (distributed) or
platform-bound on the blockchain (represented).
Tokenization can move issuance, settlement and servicing onto a shared ledger

### Page 6

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
Credit is tokenized at wrapper or asset level; most value is still represented
There are two broad approaches to tokenizing credit
❑ Wrapper-level tokenization: The underlying credit assets remain in a
conventional portfolio structure, while a fund or SPV is tokenized, e.g.
Galaxy’s new-issue CLO with debt tranches issued and tokenized on
Avalanche, and Apollo Diversified Credit (ACRED).

❑ Asset-level / native tokenization: Blockchain is integrated into the
underlying credit asset's issuance, ownership record and/or lifecycle.
Examples are Figure Technologies and Tradable, which use blockchains to
support loan administration, ownership records, payments tracking, and
servicing.
❑ Figure can be broken down into distributed vs represented, given it
can be reclassified if they become movable and transferrable.
❑ Asset-level vs wrapper-level shows what is tokenized; distributed vs

represented shows how the token can circulate.
State of tokenized securities
❑ Private credit stands ~$50bn in total (~$8bn distributed vs ~$42bn
represented). Includes asset-backed credit ($33bn), specialty finance
($13bn), corporate credit ($2bn), and diversified credit ($795mn).
❑ Use case is mostly record-keeping; growing adoption for transferable
tokenized credit funds.
❑ Tokenized securitized market is also still mostly at the fund level (i.e. KKR &

Securitize, Hamilton Lane) but efforts to tokenize underlying assets is
growing (i.e. Figure, Centrifuge, Intain). E.g. Figure Technologies is
reportedly prepping its first fully tokenized RMBS issuance in the US for
late 2026/early 2027.

### Page 7

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
Benefits: Transparency – one shared record replaces three reconciled copies
❑ Problem: Traditional securitization can be opaque. Investors often rely on
periodic, manually reconciled reports rather than direct visibility.
❑ Solution: A shared ledger for every party, tokenization allows investors to
access an audit trail time-stamped with evidence of tamper history, visible

holder register to both issuer and regulator, and loan level by default,
making it permissioned.
Potential use case examples
❑ Immutable, public records of the blockchain prevents double pledging of
assets (e.g., First Brands controversy).
❑ Illuminates circular financing (e.g., AI data centre capex buildout), where a
company provides liquidity to a second company so that it can buy its
goods/services.
❑ Commingled cash flows can obscure a company’s operating

performance and can make it more complex for an investor in
evaluating its underlying economic health and cash position.
❑ Enables more bespoke structures (i.e., multi-strategy CDOs for private
credit loan books), by mapping investor exposure to underlying assets,
helping establish correlations and credit risk that may not be immediately
apparent.

### Page 8

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
Benefits: Liquidity – platforms can build a supply-and-demand flywheel
❑ Problem: The structural complexity of the asset class can negatively
impact investor sentiment, serving as a headwind for market growth, and
ultimately impact market depth, liquidity, and prices. And the operational
complexity required for bringing a securitized deal to market can lead to

long settlement times, producing a liquidity gap for an investor.
❑ Solution: The asset transparency resulting from the use of a shared
ledger as core, underlying market infrastructure would help to draw-in
more investors that have traditionally sat on the sidelines. This has been a
hurdle for both structured credit and private credit assets.
❑ A decrease in the perceived complexity cost should naturally lead to a
cohort of net-new investors allocating to the asset class, enabling both

market growth and the added liquidity achieved from a developed
secondary market. An analog, TradFi example of this can be found in the
CLO market, where primary market growth has enabled a robust
secondary market to evolve, further supporting investor sentiment for the
asset class.  The scale achieved has been a factor in driving spreads
tighter over the last few years.
❑ The process simplification enabled by using a block-chain as
foundational infrastructure can lead to time savings, spurring demand

which then attracts greater supply.  Figure Technology’s “Connect”
Platform, a two-sided marketplace developed on the Provenance
blockchain, is an example, connecting loan originators with asset buyers.
Its HELOC offering, which lowers the time and cost required for an
originator to price a loan, has been the service which has played an
outsized role in driving market adoption – Q3 saw growth, with Figure
Connect volume reaching $4.3bn compared to Q2’s $2.8bn.

Connect is an early example of a tokenized securitization marketplace
Sources: Figure Technology Solutions financial filings, AlphaSense,, Deutsche Bank Research.

### Page 9

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
Benefits: Lower costs and faster settlement – ~50–90 bps to bring a deal to market
❑ Problem: Intermediaries (e.g., arranging banks, rating agencies,
lawyers, and trustees) provide essential services for deal activity and
the ongoing administration of cash distributions and collateral
monitoring.
❑ Yet, this costs investors and borrowers fees and processing time,

delaying when an investor receives their first cash distribution by up to
4 months.
❑ Solution: Embedding blockchain into the securitization value chain
could substitute outright (the rails and software providing the service)
or help stakeholders coordinate more efficiently, lowering transaction
costs and time to value for investors. This would also compress
settlement timelines and speed up capital flows.
❑ Net impact: As a distributed ledger, blockchain has features relevant to

securitization: immutable records ensure consistent, reliable data
permeance, and distribution gives participants greater data access.
❑ Embedding blockchain in the securitized value chain would enable four
qualities for the market that would unlock the next stage of growth:
transparency, liquidity, cost efficiency, and speed of settlement.
❑ The savings generated could bring deal costs down materially, by
upwards of 85%.

### Page 10

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026
Securitization has supported US growth; blockchain could be the next step
The size of the US Securitized market is ~45% of US GDP❑ Structured credit has been an asset class traditionally associated with
financial innovation: it turns a pool of assets that individually carry lower
credit ratings and more credit risk, into a structured pool whose senior
tranche is often AAA.

❑ And it has been an economic growth driver – at $15trn of debt
outstanding, it is roughly 20% of US nonfinancial debt outstanding, and
just north of 45% of US GDP.
❑ The next inflection point of growth for the market could come from using
blockchain technology to optimize the securitization value chain,
embedding origination, structuring, distribution, and administration on-
chain as programmable, automated services.
❑ The continued growth of stablecoins in the years ahead, in addition to US

Treasury funds will be a necessary precursor for the growth of the
tokenized, securitized markets, as it has been for the Structured credit
markets in the TradFi world.
❑ An important relative value feature that a Structured asset provides is the
yield over US government debt – as more investment flows into the DeFi
space, Tokenized Structured products will see material growth.
❑Ultimately, the market’s expansion would be a tailwind for US GDP
growth.

US US SEC stipulates that the tokenized format of credit/structured products does not change how federal securities laws apply to it.
EU
Tokenized securities fall outside MiCA. Falls entirely under EU financial

protection and trading rules).
UK
Digital sandbox, 16 firms are issuing and settling tokenized assets in the
Digital Securities sandbox. A tokenization roadmap is due later this year,
with a custody consultation in H1 2027.
Hong Kong Puts security tokens under the same regulation as traditional securities.
Singapore Project Guardian includes native issuance of asset-backed security and several tokenized private credit funds.
Japan Puts security tokens under the same regulation as traditional securities.

China Banned security tokens on shore, but off-shore tokens can have regulatory approval
❑ The tokenized RWA market remains small ($421bn in Nov 2026)
relative to traditional markets, and token issuance does not
automatically create secondary-market liquidity. Tokenized
securitized debt faces similar challenges to broader tokenized trends.
❑ Secondary-market liquidity remains low. Issuance growth has not
consistently translated into active trading or deep markets.

❑ Regulatory uncertainty: Fragmented or incomplete frameworks slow
institutional adoption and complicate integration with traditional
financial infrastructure. Legal uncertainty persists over whether token
ownership conveys ownership of the underlying asset or a claim on it.
❑ Fragmented infrastructure: Public blockchains, permissioned
institutional ledgers and hybrid platforms often lack interoperability,
fragmenting markets. Clearstream, DTCC and Euroclear published a

joint framework to address this in March 2026.
❑ Limited integration with existing custody, settlement, and record-
keeping arrangements prevents institutions from scaling tokenized
products. Proprietary infrastructure and centralized controls also
raise questions about ownership and governance.

### Page 12

Marion Laboure, Camilla Siazon, Jamie Flannick | Tokenized Securitization |  November 2026 12
Outlook: Tokenization is still infrastructure-led; gains are operational today, credit-risk and liquidity benefits come
later
Tokenized securitization still mostly used for record-keeping purposes; how
much financial activity is adopting blockchain is still small
❑ Tokenization still mostly helps with the infrastructure/plumbing layer of the
securitized market: data, servicing, settlement and collateral mobility.

❑ Hence, distributed value of the tokenized securitized market ($8bn) remains
small compared to represented ($42bn).
❑ In contrast, US Treasury debt remains the largest (ex-stablecoins) on-chain
tokenized asset segment reaching $14bn (distributed) in November 2026.
❑ Demand reflects appetite for liquid, short-term, risk-free collateral, that can
generate yield unlike illiquid segments like private credit.
Potential to transform credit risk, but increased liquidity is further out

❑ Industry is currently in the infrastructure phase. Gains are so far operational:
lower costs, faster settlement times and data transparency.
❑ Move towards tokenizing underlying assets (rather than fund-wrapper style)
has potential to effectively reduce credit risk by countering double-pledging,
settlement risks.
❑ Tokenization remains concentrated among a small number of issuers and lacks
a deep, and liquid secondary market. Distributed value is still small, illustrating

limited activity in actual borrowing demand.
❑ Near term, tokenized Treasuries and money market funds will be the first
market to scale. Longer-term opportunities for private credit, once efforts to
integrate tokenized infrastructure with legacy systems (i.e. DTCC, NYSE,
Nasdaq), money (stablecoins, tokenized deposits), and broader regulation
progresses.

《歐洲信用債市受法國財政疑慮波及》
歐洲信用市場雖受AI相關債券波動影響較小，但因
能源價格高企，加上近期法國主權和財政風險加劇，
歐洲信用債市十月份表現也有不小波動，尤其是非
投資級債信用利差一度擴大逾60個基點。歐洲銀行
債是各產業信用債主權風險曝險較高的類別，因歐
洲銀行多數不及美國銀行營運遍及全球且業務多元，
此外也持有不少主權債資產，十月份市場表現也反
映此一狀況，主權債的波動更加重了歐洲銀行債的
跌幅，法國銀行債利差擴大幅度最為明顯，所幸法
國主要銀行包括法國巴黎銀行以及興業銀行3Q26獲
利狀況良好，即使經營環境惡化，從不良貸款率與

信用風險成本等資產品質指標來看法國巴黎銀行持
平前季、法國興業銀行僅微幅上升。
目前市場關注的焦點仍是法國財政整頓的路徑，法
國目前的債務負擔約佔GDP的118%至119%，高盛
預計，如果沒有更有效的財政調整，到2030年初負
債比率將進一步上升至接近125%，如要穩定債務
佔GDP的比重，法國需要實現約佔GDP 1%的財政
盈餘，但目前的財政赤字約佔2.5% GDP。法國政
府當局可能在年底前通過一項撙節的預算案，若沒
通過法國主權債與信用債可能進一步惡化，但即使
通過法國主權債利率要大幅回落的機會仍不大，可
能要到2027年法國大選後主權債才有機會趨於穩定，
屆時法國銀行債或有布局機會。
歐洲可供吸收損失銀行債信用利差(基點)

重點摘要
• 高利率持續下信用基本面都
已 出 現分 化，如CCC等級的
償債能力指標顯著疲弱
• 衡量整體債信風險仍是歐洲
較美國為高
《非投資級債美國仍優於歐洲》
儘管目前不論是美國還是歐洲的非投資級債在高利
率持續下信用基本面都已出現分化，如CCC等級的
償債能力指標顯著疲弱，但衡量整體債信風險仍是
歐洲較美國為高；原因包括歐洲的債務到期牆較美
國明顯，且美國非投資級債2028年前到期的債券分
佈在相對廣泛的行業，而當前的賤售債券主要集中
在媒體、原物料與電信、非必需消費等特定領域，
上述較少重疊降低落入賤售債券利差區間的發行人
同時面臨債務到期後償債或再融資令違約風險增加
的可能。另一方面，歐洲非投資級債的到期牆更為
陡峭，且市場利率與2028年前到期債券的票面利率

差距大於美非投資級債，反映再融資壓力更鉅，尤
其是歐洲CCC等級。
分析歐洲非投資級債各產業債務到期狀況可發現
2028年前到期債務產業相對集中，且易受景氣影響
的非必需消費在到期債務與賤售債券都佔有一定比
例，另外槓桿率本就較高的電信業佔2028年前到期
債務9%，融資成本大幅上漲恐令這些發行人債信體
質進一步惡化。

保険業界の規制改革とインド経済
インド政府は、過去四半世紀にわたり国民生活の安定と金融仲介機能の強化に向けて保険市場の自由化を
推進してきたが、ここにきて消費者保護強化に向けた規制強化にかじを切ろうとしている。
■規制緩
和を受けてインドの保険市場は急成長
インド政府は、国民生活の安定と金融仲介機
能の強化に向けて、保険市場の発展を推進して
きた。この背景としては、以下の 2 点を指摘で
きる。
第 1 に、インドでは社会保障制度が整備途上
にある。そのため、予期せぬ疾病や事故をきっ
かけとする世帯所得の急減リスクを抑制するう
えで、医療保険や生命保険への加入が極めて重
要なセーフティネットとなる。
第 2 に、持続的なインド経済の発展のために
は、預入期間の短い銀行預金とは別に、インフ
ラ整備や産業育成の原動力となる長期かつ安定
的な国内資本の蓄積が必要である。保険会社が

集めて運用する巨額の資金は、まさにこの長期
資金供給の担い手として期待されてきた。
これらの目的の達成に向け、インド政府は過
去四半世紀にわたって段階的な規制緩和を進め
てきた（右上表）。2000 年の民間企業や外資企
業の参入自由化を皮切りに、2002 年のバンカシ
ュアランス（銀行窓口での保険販売）解禁、外
資出資比率上限の引き上げなどを進めた。
こうした一連の規制緩和によって多数の民
間・外国企業が参入し、市場競争が活性化した。
日本からも日本生命や東京海上グループを含め、
複数の保険会社が参入し、対印投資を牽引した。
市場参入や販売に関わる規制緩和に加え、中
間所得層の拡大、家計における金融リテラシー
の向上、そして保険サービスのデジタル化も相
まって、インドの保険市場は飛躍的な拡大を遂
げた。保険料収入は経済成長率を上回るペースで拡大し、近年の保険会社の運用資産残高は GDP
の約 4 分の 1 に達している（右下図）。
インド政府は、独立 100 周年を迎える 2047 年までに全国民が自らのリスクに適した保険に手

ごろな価格でアクセスできる「Insurance for All by 2047」の実現を目標に掲げており、今後もイ
ンドの保険市場は基本的に底堅い成長をたどると見込まれる。

(kumagai.shotaro@jri.co.jp）

JAPAN RESEARCH INSTITUTE
2
■当局が規制強化に舵を切る理由
一方で、急激な市場拡大の副作用として、構造
的な問題も顕在化している。最大の課題は保険販
売コストの高騰と不適切販売の横行である。保険
販売競争が激化するなか、保険会社が商業銀行や
ノンバンクといった販売代理店に対して支払う
手数料・報酬は、新規契約保険料を大幅に上回る
ペースで増加しており（右上図）、それが新規保
険料の上昇や将来の保険料支払いの減少につな
がることへの懸念が広がった。また、顧客のニー
ズに合致しない複雑な商品の強引な販売や、商品
に関する説明不足も社会問題化した。
こうした状況を重くみた IRDAI（イン
ド保険規制開発局）は、市場の健全性を
維持すべく、足元で消費者保護を主眼と
した規制強化を検討している。2026 年 9
月に公表した改革案では、①販売代理店
への報酬構造の見直し、②保険会社の事
業費・管理費に対する規制厳格化、③販

売手法に対する規制厳格化、④コンプラ
イアンスの徹底に向けた流通チャネルの
合理化、⑤監査や情報公開制度の厳格化、
などの方向性が示された（右下表）。改
革案に関する意見を広く募ったうえで、
2027 年前半に新たな制度を導入するとし
ている。
一連の規制強化の影響は、プラス面と
マイナス面に分けられる。まず、プラス
面としては、過度なインセンティブ競争
や不適切な販売が抑制されることで消費
者保護が大幅に強化され、保険市場の信
頼性と持続的発展の基盤が強化される点が挙げられる。
他方、マイナス面として懸念されるのは、移行期における副作用である。販売規制の厳格化に
伴い、保険会社および販売代理店である金融機関の収益拡大ペースの一時的な鈍化は避けられな
い。さらに、保険会社や金融機関がインドの金融市場における主たる投資主体であることを勘案
すると、規制強化が株式・債券価格に対して下押し圧力をもたらす公算が大きい。
保険市場のこうした変化は、今後のインド経済を展望するうえで重要な視点を提供する。すな

わち、保険業界に限らず多くの産業において、自由化による市場の急成長は様々な問題を生じさ
せ得るものの、そうした事態に対する規制の再設計・適正化こそが、新たな発展の礎になるとい
う点である。その嚆矢として保険をめぐる規制の行方が注目される。

生命保険

ASIA MONTHLY  2026 年 11 月（No.308） 
JAPAN RESEARCH INSTITUTE 
   3
専門人材の渡航制限が中国の成長を下押す恐れ 
中国政府は 9 月、新たな出入国管理規定を施行した。技術流出を防ぐための人材の囲い込みは、海外の知
識を取り込む機会を狭め、中長期的な経済成長を下押しする恐れがある。

■海外渡航制限の対象者を拡大 
中国政府は 9 月 15 日、出入国の条件や審査手
続きを具体化する新規定を施行した。新規定では、
輸出管理・技術輸出入の規則に違反し、国家の産
業・技術の安全を脅かす可能性がある中国国民に
対して、商務部などが出国禁止を決定できる旨が
明記された（右表）。 
中国では、2013 年に施行された出境入境管理
法に基づき、刑事事件の被告人・被疑者や、未解
決の民事事件について裁判所が出国を認めない
者などは、出国制限の対象となっていた。また、
中国国民については、国家の安全と利益を損なう
恐れがあることを理由に、関係当局が出国を認め
ない場合も規定されていた。出国禁止とは別に、
国家機密を扱う人員や公的部門の幹部らに対し、旅券の集中保管や渡航の事前承認による管理も
行われてきた。今回の新規定では、経済安全保障面での出国制限が明確化された形となる。 
こうしたなか、9 月末には、AI・半導体分野の重要人材に課されていた海外渡航制限が、配偶
者や子供などの家族にも拡大されたと報じられた。報道によれば、重要人材には、著名な AI スタ
ートアップ企業の創業者、大手民間企業の主要幹部、中核的な研究開発人材などが含まれる。一
律の出国禁止ではないものの、対象者とその家族は、短期の私的旅行や出張であっても、出国に
当局の許可が必要とされる。 
今回の規制強化の背景には、米中間の技術競争があると考えられる。生成 AI などの重要技術を
巡る競争が激しくなるなか、中国政府は、技術だけでなく、それを生み出す研究者や技術者も戦
略的な資産として重視しているとみられる。家族の渡航も管理することで、本人の海外移住や研
究開発拠点の移転を抑制する狙いがあると考えられる。今回の報道で注目されるのは、公的部門
に限らず、民間企業の重要人材やその家族にも管理が及んでいる点である。今回のような人材移
動の制約は、これまで中国の成長を支えてきた技術吸収と生産性向上のメカニズムを揺るがす恐
れがある。 
■市場開放と技術吸収に支えられた中国の生産性上昇 
経済成長に対して、生産性は大きな影響力を持つ。一国経済の生産効率を示す全要素生産性
（Total Factor Productivity, TFP ）は、資本や労働の投入量だけでは説明できない生産の効率性
を捉える推計指標であり、技術水準や資源配分の効率性などを反映する。 
中国の TFP は、長期的に上昇してきた（次頁右上図）。背景には、改革開放後の市場化や民間
企業の成長に加え、貿易・直接投資を通じて、海外の技術や経営ノウハウを取り込んできたこと
が指摘できる。加えて、農業から製造業などへの労働移動や、企業間の競争・新陳代謝も、経済

全体の生産性向上を支えてきた。
もっとも、中国の TFP は主要国のなかでは依
然として低い水準にある（右下図）。Penn World
Table の推計によると、2023 年の中国の TFP は
米国の半分弱であり、ドイツをはじめとする欧
州主要国、韓国や日本はもとより、タイやマレ
ーシアの水準にも届いていない。背景には、非
効率な資源配分や先進的な技術・経営手法の普
及の遅れが挙げられる。国有企業への金融・制
度面での優遇、地方保護主義、企業の参入・退
出を妨げる仕組みなどが資源配分を歪め、資本
や労働が生産性の高い企業・部門で十分に活用
されていない。また、中国が電気自動車や半導
体などの一部の先端分野で高い競争力を持って
いても、そうした産業の先進的な技術・経営手
法が経済の大宗を担うサービス業や中小企業な
どには普及していないことも、全体の生産性向
上を制約する要因となっている。

■技術の囲い込みが中長期的に成長を阻害
技術流出を防ぐための人材の囲い込みは、生
産性の先進国へのキャッチアップを一層遅らせ
る恐れがある。専門人材の渡航制限には、国内
の研究開発人材を確保し、既存の技術的優位を
守る側面がある一方、海外の知識を吸収したり、
新たな技術を海外と共同で生み出したりする機会を狭める可能性がある。技術やノウハウの移転
は、論文や設計図といった「形式知」の共有だけで完結するものではなく、対面での議論や共同
作業、生産現場での経験を通じて共有される「暗黙知」が極めて重要である。渡航管理によって
国際学会への参加や共同研究、海外企業との技術交流が遅延・見送りとなれば、こうした知識を
吸収する機会が失われ、技術革新や生産工程の改善が遅れる恐れがある。さらに、渡航許可の取
得に時間がかかったり、許可の見通しが立ちにくくなったりすれば、技術者の海外派遣や国際的
な人材配置の障害にもなる。
今回施行された新規定は外国人の入国管理も対象としており、出入国管理の運用への不透明感

が高まれば、海外からの高度人材の招聘や定着にとっても障壁となる。すでに非効率な資源配分
や先進的な知見の普及の遅れが中国経済の生産性向上の重石となっているなか、海外との人的交
流まで制約されれば、知識の吸収・創出と、その活用がさらに滞る可能性がある。影響の大きさ
は対象範囲や実際の運用に左右されるものの、技術保護を過度に重視した人材の交流や渡航に関
する制限は中長期的に TFP の伸びを鈍らせ、中国経済の潜在成長率を下押しする逆風となり得る。

5
台 湾 AI ブームを背景に高成長が継続
■足元は内外需ともに好調
台湾景気は、内外需に支えられて拡大している。
外需の面では、世界的な AI 需要の高まりを受け
て米国向けの半導体や AI サーバーの輸出が急増
しており、輸出全体は前年同月比＋50％程度で推
移している。一方で、停滞が指摘されていた AI
ブームの恩恵を受けにくいその他製品の輸出も、
足元では 2 桁の伸びを見せている（右上図）。た
だし、その主因は輸出品の価格上昇である。実際、
それら製品を製造する産業の生産活動は横ばい
にとどまっており、本格的な回復とは言えない状
況である。
個人消費は、良好な雇用・所得環境に支えられ
て好調に推移している。製造業では、AI ブームに
押し上げられている電子部品産業のほか、幅広い
製造業において高水準の賃上げが行われている。
さらに、株価の上昇による資産効果も消費を押し

上げており、台湾の代表的な株価指数である加権
指数は、年初から 10 月初旬までに＋60％上昇し
た。これらを背景に、小売売上高は、主に生活必
需品を販売する一般店、嗜好品を多く扱う専門店ともに高水準の伸びを維持している（右下図）。
投資も拡大している。AI ブームを背景に、電子部品産業による生産能力の増強投資が活発化し
ており、同産業が台湾全体の設備投資や工場建設増加の 9 割以上を占めている。
■2026 年の実質 GDP 成長率は＋10％超へ
先行きの台湾経済を展望すると、輸出と投資を両輪とする景気拡大が続き、2026 年の実質 GDP
成長率は＋11.1％に達すると予想する。2 桁の成長率は、16 年ぶりとなる。
輸出は引き続き増加する見込みである。海外のデータセンター投資の拡大を背景に、半導体や
サーバーの輸出は好調を維持すると予想する。
投資も好調を維持する見通しである。半導体や AI サーバーのメーカーは相次いで今年の設備投
資計画を上方修正しているほか、関連部材の製造業などでも能力増強投資が拡大している。

台湾経済の主な下振れリスクは、AI 投資の収益性への懸念が高まり、AI ブームが調整局面を迎
えることである。AI サーバーに用いられる GPU は 3～6 年程度で陳腐化する可能性があり、買い
替えによる投資負担の増大が、収益性を悪化させるとの指摘もある。仮に世界の AI インフラ投資
のペースが鈍化すれば、AI 関連偏重の台湾の輸出と投資を大きく下押しすると考えられる。
また、物価高による消費下押しもリスク要因である。足元の消費者物価の前年同月比は、中央
銀行の警戒ラインである＋2％を上回って推移している。①エネルギー価格の高止まり、②パソコ
ンや携帯電話などの半導体搭載製品の価格高騰、③積極的な賃上げを背景とするサービス価格の
上昇など、足元で見られるインフレ圧力の先行きに注意が必要である。
研究員 今川
冬馬（imagawa.thoma@jri.co.jp）

6
フィリピン インフレは一段と加速
■物価高が景気を下押し
フィリピン景気は、外需は好調に推移している
ものの、内需の低迷を補いきれない状況が続いて
いる。
外需の面では、8 月の財輸出が前年同月比＋
27.8％と、前月の同＋10.8％から大幅に加速した
（右上図）。世界的な AI 関連需要の拡大を背景
に、電子機器・部品がけん引役となっている。輸
出が極めて好調であることから、7 月まで悪化し
ていた貿易収支にも、足元では改善の兆しがみら
れる。
これに対し、内需は依然として力強さを欠いて
いる。中東危機を背景とするエネルギー価格の高
止まりに加え、台風や豪雨による食品価格の上昇
が、家計の実質購買力を圧迫している。9 月の消
費者物価指数（CPI）は同＋7.2％と、8 月の同＋
6.1％から伸びが加速し、中銀の目標レンジ上限
である＋4％を大きく上回った（右下図）。

価格転嫁の動きは幅広い財・サービスに及んで
おり、変動の大きい食品とエネルギーを除くコア
CPI も同＋4.7％と、前月の同＋4.1％から上昇し
ている。背景には、ペソ安に伴う輸入コストの上
昇が挙げられる。フィリピン・ペソは 2 月末から本稿執筆時点までに、対ドルで▲9.2％下落した。
こうしたなか、中銀はインフレ抑制を優先し、4 月以降、3 会合連続で利上げを実施し、政策金
利は 5.0％の高水準となっている。インフレ率が目標レンジを大幅に上回るなか、今後も金融引き
締め姿勢を維持するとみられる。
■公共投資の回復に遅れ
内需の低迷は家計部門にとどまらない。昨年の洪水対策事業を巡る汚職問題を受け、政府が事
業点検や支払い審査を厳格化したため、4～6 月期の公共投資は前年に比べて大幅に減少した。足
元でもインフラ関連支出の回復は鈍く、景気の下押し要因となっている。10 月には、国会議員や
公共事業道路省職員、建設業者といった関係者の訴追が始まるなど、汚職事件を巡る捜査が本格
化している。こうした一連の動きは、中長期的に公共事業の健全化につながる一方、短期的には

工事の再開を遅らせ、投資を冷え込ませるとみられる。
先行き、堅調な AI 関連需要を背景に電子機器・部品の輸出は底堅く推移するとみられる一方、
内需は引き続き力強さを欠く見通しである。公共投資の停滞に加え、インフレ高進による実質購
買力の低下や、政策金利の高止まりによる借入コストの上昇が、個人消費や民間投資を抑制する
とみられる。
研究員 呉 子婧（wu.zijing@jri.co.jp）

ASIA MONTHL

JAPAN RESEARCH INSTITUTE
7
タ イ 輸出主導で回復も、消費に弱さ
■個人消費の回復は緩慢
タイ景気は、AI・テクノロジー関連需要の拡
大を背景に輸出と民間投資が想定を上回る一方、
個人消費は力強さを欠くなど、需要項目によっ
て勢いにばらつきが見られる。
輸出は高い伸びを維持している。8 月の財輸出
は前年同月比＋24.3％と、前月の同＋23.9％から
伸びが加速した。世界的な AI 関連需要の拡大を
受けて、電子機器・部品が輸出をけん引してい
る。民間投資も増勢を強めている。データセン
ターや電子・半導体関連設備への投資拡大が主因となっている。
これに対し、個人消費の回復は緩慢である。政府による一連の消費支援策を受け、ホテル・レ
ストランなどのサービス消費や耐久財支出は増加したものの、家計の節約志向は依然として根強
い。9 月の消費者信頼感指数は 52.1 と、中立水準の 100 を大きく下回り、本格的な改善には至っ

ていない（右上図）。背景には、GDP 比 85％を超える家計債務の返済負担が可処分所得を圧迫し
ていることに加え、中小企業向け融資の減少により雇用・所得環境の改善が遅れ、企業部門好調
の恩恵が家計に十分波及していないことがある。
■物価上昇と洪水被害が景気の下押し要因に
こうしたなか、足元では物価上昇も家計消費
の重石となっている。9 月の消費者物価指数は前
年同月比＋2.8％と、8 月の同＋2.5％から伸びが
加速した（右下図）。中東情勢の緊迫化に伴う
エネルギー価格の高止まりと、天候不順による
生鮮食品価格の上昇が主因である。
9 月以降の洪水被害の深刻化も、さらなる景気
下押し要因となっている。タイ防災減災局
（DDPM）は 10 月 4 日、洪水の影響が約 120
万世帯、326 万人以上に及んだと発表した。洪
水は農地や物流網にも被害をもたらしており、農産物の出荷停滞を通じて食品価格を一段と押し
上げる懸念がある。企業活動への影響も広がっている。タイ工業連盟（FTI）によると、工業部門

の損失額は 10 月初旬時点で約 60 億バーツに上り、とりわけ自動車産業では部品供給への懸念が
高まっている。被害が長期化すれば、部品不足や輸送コストの高騰を通じて企業の生産活動が下
押しされる可能性がある。さらに、家屋や設備の復旧に向けた政府支出の増加は、短期的には景
気を下支えする一方、中長期的な財政運営上の負担となる可能性もある。
以上を踏まえると、需要面と供給面の両方からの下押し圧力が強まることで、先行きの景気は
力強さを欠く展開が続く見通しである。2026 年の実質 GDP 成長率は＋1.8％と、前年の＋2.4％
から減速すると予想する。
研究員 呉 子婧（wu.zijing@jri.co.jp）

JAPAN RESEARCH INSTITUTE
8
中 国 景気減速にようやく歯止め
■7～9 月期の成長率は持ち直し
中国景気は減速に歯止めがかかった。本年 7～
9 月期の実質 GDP は前年同期比＋4.5％と、前期
から若干持ち直した（右上図）。
押し上げの主因は、好調な輸出である。7～9
月期のドル建て輸出額は同＋28.4％と、4～6 月
期（同＋20.1％）に比べて伸びが加速した。地域
別では、ASEAN や EU 向けが好調に推移した。
米国向けも、両国の経済関係の改善の流れを受け、
前年同月比プラスが続いている。品目別では、
EV を含む自動車、AI ブームを背景とした IT 関連財がけん引した。
一方、内需は引き続き低迷している。固定資産投資は、過剰生産や不動産不況といった構造的
な下押し要因が残存し、総じて低調であった。とくに、不動産開発投資は前年同期比でマイナス
幅が拡大している。政府が製造業高度化の象徴として重視するハイテク製造業など、一部の業種

では堅調に拡大したものの、投資全体を押し上げるには至っていない。
消費は、財を中心に弱含んでいる。7～9 月期の小売売上高は同＋0.7％と、前期からほぼ横ばい
で推移した。背景には、①自動車が買い替え補助金による需要の反動減と、原油高によるガソリ
ン車需要の減退から大幅減となったこと、②不動産不況を受け、家具・建材などの売上の不振が
続いていること、の 2 点がある。旅行や外食は、9 月末の大型連休期間中は需要が増加したものの、
一過性の持ち直しに終わった。
■先行き、景気刺激策が押し上げ要因に
こうした内需の低迷を受け、政府は 9 月末以降、
追加対策を発表している（右下表）。景気浮揚効
果の観点で注目される措置としては、まず住宅ロ
ーンの利子補給が挙げられる。また、金融緩和を
通じて、ハイテク分野への投資や次世代通信網・
物流網の整備といった「六つのネットワーク」を
推進しようとしている。これらの取り組みは、住
宅関連需要の持ち直しや、ハイテク製造業とイン
フラ部門の投資拡大などをもたらし、内需を押し

上げると見込まれる。加えて、外需も当面は好調
を維持するため、10～12 月期の成長率は 7～9
月期を若干上回る見通しである。その結果、2026
年通年では政府目標（＋4.5～5.0％）をかろうじ
て達成できる見込みである。
もっとも、それを踏まえても内需は自律的な回復には至らないとみられ、政策効果が薄れる 2027
年入り後に、景気は再び減速すると予想される。
主任研究員 佐野 淳也（sano.junya@jri.co.jp）

## Document 531: 債券市場總評：製藥債穩中轉強

Source PDF: all report pdf/債券市場總評：製藥債穩中轉強.pdf
Report source: Fubon
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1 
*上述相關標的僅舉例說明，非單一標的推薦。 
台北富邦銀行 
Nov 13，2026 | 3:00 PM (GMT+8) 
債券市場總評：製藥債穩中轉強
投資研究部 
 Research 
Financial Market 
▎就業與通膨具韌性，升息預期升溫 
自 11 月以來，美債殖利率先降後升。期中選舉後，民
主黨拿下國會兩院，財政擴張可能受到更多制衡，加
上科技股大跌與美伊情勢一度降溫，帶動避險需求升
高、長端殖利率回落。不過，10 月非農新增 16.5 萬
人、失業率降至 4.1%，CPI 與核心 CPI 月增率分別為
0.4%與 0.3%，雙雙高於預期，加上多位 Fed 官員言
論轉趨鷹派，推升 12 月升息預期機率至 100%。

圖 1  10 月非農優於預期，CPI 月增偏高

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/11/12 
▎中東情勢反覆，油價回升壓抑債市 
近日川普表示美國無須與伊朗達成協議，使市場對美
伊情勢持續降溫的期待減弱，國際油價再度反彈，通
膨疑慮隨之升高。加上市場對後續升息的預期轉強，
10 年期美債殖利率由 11 月初低點 4.83%快速反彈至
5.13%，重新升破 5%。在地緣政治與利率風險反覆
下，市場波動仍高，隨殖利率快速回升，近期債券價
格再度回落，債市再度轉趨承壓。

圖 2 殖利率隨油價反彈，美國公債、投等債同步回落

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/11/12 
◼ 期中選舉民主黨贏得國會兩院，政策制衡預期升高，油價回落、避險需求升溫，
殖利率走低。不過，10 月非農與 CPI 高於預期，Fed 官員偏鷹，12 月升息預期
升溫。中東情勢反覆、油價回升，10 年期殖利率反彈至 5.13%，債市轉趨承壓。 
◼ 近期製藥債利差彈幅低於整體投等債，防禦性較佳。醫療債升評潛力中，近 70%
集中製藥業，3Q 醫療 EPS 超預期幅度居 11 大產業之首。2027 年製藥次產業 EPS
預估年增 24.2%，高於醫療保健與 S&P500 整體，製藥業獲利信用趨勢同步轉強。 
◼ 3Q 多數大型藥廠營運優於預期，現金流充足，槓桿維持健康水準。禮來*與嬌生
*財務與營運穩健，諾和諾德*具成長動能。默克*現金流與槓桿改善，但仍有產品
集中風險。輝瑞*與必治妥施貴寶*則面臨較大專利到期與產品接替壓力。在獲利
與財務條件支撐下，大型製藥債仍具配置價值。

2026 年初至今報酬率 
美國非投等債報酬率 0.67% 
美國公債報酬率 -1.47% 
美國投等債報酬率 -1.78%

美國 10 年公債殖利率 5.13% 
布蘭特原油期貨 $96/桶 
美國 10 年期公債殖利率 5.13% 
美國基準利率 3.75~4% 
10 月 CPI 月增率 0.4% 
10 月核心 CPI 月增率 0.3% 
10 月非農就業新增 16.5 萬人 
10 月失業率 4.1%

### Page 2

2

誠信親切、專業創新 週報 
▎製藥債利差展現韌性，升評潛力突出 
近期美債殖利率回升、整體債市重新承壓，但信用市場
內部的產業表現仍明顯分化。4Q 以來，美國投資等級
債 OAS 約擴大 7.6bps，相較之下，醫療保健製藥僅擴
大約 2.1bps，醫療保健整體亦僅約 3.5bps，與必需消
費、公用事業等防禦性產業相近，並明顯低於科技與通
訊產業的利差擴張幅度。在信用市場波動升高的環境下，
製藥債利差表現仍展現相對較佳的防禦性。

圖 3 近期製藥等防禦性產業 OAS 彈幅相對小

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/11/10

製藥債相對穩健的利差表現，也有信用評級趨勢支撐。
目前醫療保健投等債中，約有 18%具升評潛力，規模約
2,280 億美元，其中製藥業占近 70%，為信用評級改善
潛力最集中的領域。過去數年部分藥廠因併購提高槓桿，
隨著後續債務削減與資產負債表改善，信用指標逐步修
復，評級上調空間也隨之增加。整體而言，製藥債近期
兼具相對穩定的利差表現與信用改善潛力，基本面在醫
療保健產業中相對突出。 
圖 4 醫療升評潛力債務規模中，製藥債占近 7 成

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/11/9 
▎3Q 醫療獲利超預期，製藥成長將加速 
醫療保健近期獲利表現強勁。2026 年第 3 季醫療保健
EPS 高於市場預期幅度約 14%，居 S&P500 11 大產業
之首，亦明顯較 S&P500 的 4%高。公用事業、能源與
工業等產業雖同樣優於預期，但幅度仍低於醫療保健，
反映近期醫療企業獲利表現具有相對優勢。製藥業第 3 
季營運亦維持強勁，多家公司營收優於預期並上調財測，
加上新藥逐步接替成熟產品線、藥價政策風險減退，進
一步支撐大型藥廠獲利展望。 
圖 5 3Q 醫療保健獲利超預期幅度居 11 大產業之冠

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/11/10

彭博預估，2027 年 S&P500 企業 EPS 將年增 18.7%，
其中醫療保健產業預估年增約 19.8%，在 11 大產業中
僅次於資訊技術。進一步來看，製藥次產業 EPS 預估年
增約 24.2%，且較 2026 年大幅回升，成長改善幅度最
為突出。在多數產業成長動能放緩的情況下，製藥業獲
利表現相對強勁。 
圖 6 2027 年製藥次產業獲利預期成長動能強勁

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/11/10 
美國投等債 OAS: +7.6 bps 
標普 500 企業: +4%

### Page 3

3

誠信親切、專業創新 週報 
▎大型藥廠現金流穩，多數維持低槓桿 
大型製藥公司具備穩健的現金流基礎。2026 年主要藥
廠預估自由現金流普遍具一定規模，其中禮來*與嬌生*
分別約 237 億與 224 億美元，輝瑞*、默克*與必治妥
施貴寶*亦約達 132～134 億美元。大型藥廠在維持一
定營運規模的同時，仍能產生可觀自由現金流，提供債
務償還與資本運用所需的財務彈性。

圖 7  主要藥廠 2026 年預估營收、自由現金流量

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/11/10 
*上述相關標的僅舉例說明，非單一標的推薦。

從獲利能力與槓桿來看，諾和諾德*與禮來*EBITDA 利
潤率分別約 49%與 46%，淨槓桿約 0.7x 與 1.0x，嬌生
*淨槓桿亦約 0.8x。相較之下，輝瑞*與默克*淨槓桿約
達 2.7x 與 2.5x。整體來看，多數大型藥廠淨槓桿仍維
持低至中度水準。

圖 8 主要藥廠 EBITDA 利潤率與淨槓桿

現金流對債務比率均超過 50%，必治妥施貴寶*約 48%，

表 1 主要製藥大廠的財務比率

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/11

▎醫療防禦性佳，製藥信用轉強 
近期市場資金轉向防禦性產業，醫療保健受惠於獲利
表現改善及政策風險減輕，產業表現明顯轉強，其中
製藥業的營運與信用趨勢相對突出。 
大型藥廠中，禮來*受惠於肥胖症與糖尿病業務快速成
長，現金流持續增加且槓桿預期進一步下降。嬌生*則
由製藥業務穩健成長及較低槓桿支撐，財務體質相對
穩健。諾和諾德*仍具有肥胖症業務成長動能，但面臨
價格與競爭壓力。默克*現金流與槓桿趨勢改善，近期
研發進展亦有助擴充產品線，但核心產品營收占比仍
高，產品集中風險尚未完全消除。輝瑞*與必治妥施貴
寶*近期營運雖優於預期，未來數年仍面臨較大的專利
到期與產品接替壓力。整體而言，製藥信用仍偏正
面，大型製藥債在獲利、現金流與財務結構支撐下，
仍具配置價值。

## Document 564: 凱基投顧_9914 美利達_莊政翰_20261117

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_9914 美利達_莊政翰_20261117.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十一月 17, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 自行車 ‧ 台灣

美利達 
(9914.TW/9914 TT)

邁入新成長週期 
重要訊息 
我們參加美利達線上法說會，並看好 2027 年三大關鍵成長動能：(1) 更多新
車款推出，尤其是電動自行車；(2) 中國市場維持穩健成長；以及 (3) 美國轉
投資 Specialized Bicycle Components (SBC ，美) 獲利能力改善。上述因素
支撐我們對美利達營收與獲利重拾成長動能的看法。 
評論及分析 
營收將重拾成長動能。美利達今年以來台灣廠出貨量年減 23%，主因公司
刻意控制供給，以避免通路進一步面臨庫存去化壓力。隨著歐洲子公司庫存
預估年底前恢復正常水位，管理層表示自有品牌與 SBC 的新車款將於 1Q-
2Q27 擴大推出，顯示出貨量最快有望於明年 1 月重返年增軌道。加上電動
自行車佔比提升帶動產品組合優化，我們認為即將到來的新品週期將推動
2027 年營收重回成長，並透過產品組合改善與營業槓桿支撐利潤率擴張。 
SBC 4Q26有機會轉虧為盈。儘管 SBC 前三季稅前淨利年增 42% (若排除美
國關稅退稅則年增 20%)，但由於有效稅率偏高，美利達仍認列 2.8 億元的
投資損失。隨著 SBC 美國營運持續改善，加上額外 500 萬美元的關稅退稅
挹注，我們認為所得稅費用存在迴轉空間，有望帶動 SBC 4Q26 重返獲利，
並對美利達獲利帶來顯著挹注。展望未來，SBC 計畫 1Q-2Q27 推出多款新
型電動自行車與公路車，可望支撐營收復甦並進一步推升利潤率。我們預估
SBC 4Q26 及 2027 年將分別貢獻獲利 1.5 億元及 3.3 億元，較去年虧損狀態
明顯改善。 
獲利復甦將帶動評價上修。美利達 3Q26 每股盈餘 0.59 元，低於預期，主
因庫存持續去化與歐元貶值導致利潤率落後，加上 SBC 虧損較大所致。雖然
現處新車款推出前的過渡期間，短期營收動能可能仍偏疲弱，但受惠 SBC 有
望轉虧為盈，我們預期 4Q26 獲利將重返年增軌道。展望 2027 年，隨著歐
洲與中國市場需求回溫，加上傳統自行車與電動車新車款擴大推出，可望帶
動營收重拾成長並推升利潤率擴張。我們預估 2026-28 年獲利年複合成長率
將達 25%，並認為獲利與利潤率趨勢好轉，將有助推升評價上修。 
投資建議 
我們看好美利達營收即將迎來復甦，並預期利潤率與獲利結構改善將帶動評
價上修空間。我們維持「增加持股」評等及 104 元目標價，係根據 2027 年
平均每股盈餘預估 18 倍。 
投資風險 
下檔風險包括中國復甦放緩與新台幣升值。

圖 4: 公司概況  圖 5: 歐洲為主要營收來源 
公司成立於 1972 年，為台灣第二大自行車廠商，僅次於巨大 
(9921 TT, NT$ 83.1, 增加持股)。旗下產品為自有品牌「美利
達」與兩個設計代工 (ODM) 品牌 Specialized Bicycle 
Components ( 美國) 與 Centurion ( 德國)。除台中總部外，公
司在中國大陸深圳、江蘇與山東設有工廠。台灣大部分產品均
為出口，2025年大陸內銷市場佔 16%營收。

## Document 567: 凱基投顧_HD Home Depot_賴偉中_20261118

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_HD Home Depot_賴偉中_20261118.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十一月 18, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 家居裝修‧美國

Home Depot 
(HD.US/HD US)

同店成長加速，房市逆風下持續擴張市占  
重要訊息 
公司公布 3Q26 財報，稀釋後 EPS 為 3.73 元、YoY+2.8%，大致符合市場共
識。 
評論及分析 
3Q26 財報符合預期，可比同店成長加速為亮點。Home Depot  3Q26 營收
433.98 億元、YoY+4.9%，調整後營業利益 54.79 億元、YoY+2.4%，稀釋
後 EPS 為 3.73 元、YoY+2.8%。毛利率 32.8%、YoY-0.64ppts，調整後營
益率 12.6%、YoY-0.32ppts。3Q26 調整後營業利益及稀釋後 EPS 分別低於
市場共識 2.3%、0.5%，整體來說大致符合市場共識。本季財報亮點為可比
同店銷售成長加速至 2.2%、較 2Q26 成長 0.5 個百分點，相較房市交易量
及家居裝修低迷的市場環境，顯示公司正持續取得市佔率。 
房市低迷未見改善，專注投入具成長性利基項目以維持成長。過去三個月來
美國房貸利率由 6.7%攀升至 7.1%，年化成屋銷售由 393 萬棟降至 375 萬
棟，創 2010 年以來新低，房市低迷狀況未見改善。不過即使環境逆風，公
司仍持續投入於具成長性的利基項目，以維持成長並擴張市占，包括 1.)專
業市場通路，強化先前併購 SRS 與自有 HD S upply 之交叉銷售、2.)線上銷
售通路，由實體門市展示基本款樣品，線上虛擬陳列不同尺寸與顏色的選
項、3.)擴大快速配送與隔日達的服務範圍和時間。 
投資建議 
目前 Home Depot 股價約交易於 2027 年 22 倍本益比，位於過去五年歷史
區間 16~28 倍之中緣。考量總體環境未見顯著改善，且 2027 年無 IEEPA 退
稅後，能否維持同店成長仍待觀察。我們維持 Home Depot 「持有」投資評
等，目標價微調至 368 元，基於 2027 年 24 倍本益比計算而得。 
投資風險 
美國利率政策、美國關稅政策、通膨、極端氣候、美伊戰爭。

圖 3: 公司概況  圖 4：全球門店數量 
HomeDepot 成立於 1978 年，為全球最大的家具裝修零售商，集
團員工總數超過 46.5 萬人，並被納入道瓊工業成分指數及標普
500 成分指數。截至 2025 財年底公司於全球擁有超過 2,350 家
門市，平均門市面積為 10.5 萬平方英尺並銷售超過 35,000 種商
品，門市主要集中於美國、加拿大、墨西哥。除實體門市外也設
有線上商店，並銷售超過 100 萬種商品。Home Depot 主要競爭
同業為 Lowe’s。

酬。 
 持有 (N) 對個股持中性看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現符合凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總報
酬。 
 降低持股 (U) 對個股持負面看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現低於凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總報
酬。

## Document 570: 凱基投顧_Takeaway_1102 亞泥_莊政翰_20261116

### Page 1

十一月 16, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 1

線上法人說明會重點摘要

亞泥 
(1102 TT, NT$35.4, 未評等)

焦點內容 
1. 1Q-3Q26台灣水泥需求年減 13%，主要受房市信用管制及土方新制影
響，建商推案與施工進度放緩。惟自 3Q26 末起需求跌幅逐步收斂，
隨房貸及購地限制鬆綁訊號釋出、土方清運量能恢復，加上科技廠辦
建設支撐，公司認為市場最差時期已過。 
2. 1Q-3Q26 中國水泥需求年減約 10%，全年預估下滑 8-10%，惟亞泥
中國前三季銷量僅年減 7%，優於整體市場。3Q26 受煤價上漲影響，
水泥噸毛利由人民幣 17 元降至 7 元。展望 4Q26，隨煤價漲勢趨緩、
售價調漲及錯峰生產改善，噸毛利有望回升。 
3. 嘉惠電廠二期增購協議 2026 年 10 月底正式生效，發電調度比率由
30-50% 提升至 50-70%，以區間中位數估算，發電量可望增加約
50%，自 4Q26 後半季開始貢獻，2027 年效益將更為顯著。 
結論 
3Q26 兩岸水泥業務受需求疲弱及煤炭成本上漲拖累，但電力事業提供穩
定獲利，加上權益法投資收益成長，部分抵銷水泥本業衰退對整體獲利的
影響。儘管最差狀況已過，利潤率及獲利回升空間有限將壓抑股價動能。

台灣水泥營運概況 
 3Q26 台灣水泥營收年減近 15%，主要受市場需求疲弱影響出貨，
煤炭成本上漲亦壓縮獲利。由於上半年仍有低價煤炭庫存緩衝，成
本壓力自第三季起逐步反映，導致毛利率及營業利益同步下滑。 
 1Q-3Q26 台灣水泥市場需求年減 13%，主要受到房市信用管制及
年初土方新制影響，導致建商推案趨於保守、施工進度放緩。 
 自 3Q26 末起，台灣水泥市場需求跌幅逐步收斂，六都房屋買賣移
轉棟數截至 10 月已回升至持平水準，政府亦釋出房貸及建商購地
限制鬆綁訊號，土方清運量能逐漸恢復。儘管住宅需求仍偏弱，工
業區及科技廠辦建設持續提供支撐，公司認為台灣水泥市場最差時
期已過。 
 台灣煤炭主要自澳洲採購，近期成本上漲主要反映國際煤價走高，
惟受到進口水泥競爭及市場結構影響，公司難以因短期燃料價格波
動頻繁調漲售價，後續仍需與客戶協商成本轉嫁。 
 公司預估 2026 年台灣水泥業務碳費支出約 5,400 萬元。由於獨立
發電廠僅需針對廠內自用電繳納碳費，嘉惠電廠負擔極低，因此碳
費支出主要來自水泥業務。

### Page 2

台灣 亞泥

十一月 16, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 2

中國水泥營運概況 
 中國水泥需求持續處於下行週期，1Q-3Q26 全國需求年減約 10% 
至 11.3 億噸。受兩岸水泥市場需求疲弱影響，亞泥同期合併營收
年減 5.2%至 504.1 億元。 
 3Q26 中國水泥需求年減約 11%，華東地區出貨亦受強降雨影響。
公司預估 2026 年全年需求年減 8-10%，惟亞泥中國前三季銷量僅
年減約 7%，表現優於整體市場，市佔率維持穩定；2027 年噸毛
利預期與 2026 年水準相近。 
 3Q26 中國水泥平均售價季增人民幣 4 元至每噸 189 元，惟漲幅不
足以抵銷煤炭成本上升壓力。受產地安檢、檢修及冬季備貨需求影
響，煤價升至每噸人民幣 830 元 (去年同期 640 元)，導致水泥噸毛
利由人民幣 17 元降至 7 元 (已排除資產減損影響)。 
 展望 4Q26，隨煤炭供給恢復、煤價漲勢趨緩，搭配水泥業者調漲
售價及錯峰生產改善，公司預期噸毛利將較 3Q26 回升，惟仍面臨
煤炭成本壓力。 
 亞泥中國持續推動產能置換，關閉湖北亞東三條舊有產線，將產能
配額轉移至黃岡二線，預計 4Q26 投產，以提升生產效率及改善成
本結構。3Q26 因認列一次性舊產線資產減損人民幣 1.2 億元，導
致營業利益下滑。 
 中國水泥產業中長期預期維持 L 型底部盤整，隨碳交易制度逐步推
進，將擴大業者間成本差距，有望加速落後產能退出並改善產業供
需結構。惟 2026 年碳配額仍相對寬鬆，短期成本影響有限。 
 3Q26 中國熟料配合錯峰生產暫停外銷；骨料受需求疲弱及華東礦
山產能釋放影響，價格與毛利率同步下滑，惟毛利率仍約 20%，
優於水泥業務。貿易業務拓展海外分銷，已帶來穩定獲利貢獻。

電力事業 
 嘉惠電力 3Q26 營收年增約 17%，主要反映天然氣成本上漲帶動售
電價格調升，惟燃氣採購成本同步增加，使營業利益由去年同期
7.8 億元降至 7.3 億元。 
 嘉惠二期增購協議 2026 年 10 月底生效，發電調度比率由 30-50%
提升至 50-70%，以區間中位數估算，發電量可望增加約 50%，預
計自 4Q26 下半季開始貢獻，2027 年獲利貢獻將更為顯著。 
 嘉惠一期獲利仍受天然氣價格波動影響，二期則採類似電力代工模
式，天然氣採購成本可透過售電價格轉嫁，因此獲利主要取決於實
際發電調度量。 
 隨台灣 AI 及半導體產業帶動用電需求成長，公司將透過提高嘉惠
二期發電調度量，擴大電力事業獲利貢獻。

### Page 3

台灣 亞泥

十一月 16, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 3

不銹鋼及其他業務 
 遠龍不鏽鋼業務受市場需求疲弱、庫存調整及原料價格波動影響，
營運持續承壓；由於業務以冷軋及裁切加工為主，產品附加價值及
毛利率相對較低。 
 海外市場方面，新加坡水泥業務佔整體營收比重有限，雖然下半年
需求較上半年改善，但受去年同期高基期影響，年增率略為下滑，
整體營運維持平穩。

3Q26 財報 
 3Q26 營收 177.5 億元 (YoY -0.8%)，毛利率 9.8% (YoY -6.9 個百
分點)，主要受到兩岸水泥需求疲弱及煤炭成本上漲影響，本業獲利
承壓。 
 3Q26 本業獲利疲弱，主要仰賴業外收益支撐，其中權益法認列投
資收益年增 40.2%至 16.0 億元，加上股利收入 5.5 億元，部分抵
銷金融資產評價損失約 6.1 億元，惟獲利仍年減 36.7%至 24.0 億
元，換算每股盈餘為 0.68 元 (YoY -36.4%)。 
 3Q26 權益法投資收益成長主要受惠遠東新世紀處分土地資產帶來
一次性獲利，以及裕民航運受地緣政治影響、散裝航運運力縮減帶
動運價上漲，抵銷山水水泥因中國水泥景氣低迷造成的負面影響。 
 3Q26 營收部門別佔比：台灣水泥 29.6%、中國水泥 27.7%、電力
33.1%、不鏽鋼 3.2%、其他業務 6.4%。 
 受黃岡二線建設帶動，資本支出有所增加，惟公司整體自由現金流
仍維持正數。 
 公司股利配發率提升至 88%，優於同業水準，隱含股息殖利率約
5.7%。 
 3Q26 ROE 為 5.4% (對比 2025 年 4.9%)。

## Document 574: 凱基投顧_Takeaway_2636 台驊控股_賴偉中_20261113

### Page 1

十一月 13, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

法說重點摘要

台驊控股 
(2636 TT, NT$71.0, 未評等)

重點摘要 
1. 台驊 3Q26 EPS 3.73 元、YoY+30.32%，成長主要來自海運價格提
升帶動。AI 出口暢旺帶動台灣航空貨運需求強勁成長，不過台驊空
運客戶主要是近程線的供應鏈，正積極佈局長程線。 
2. 進入淡季，美線運價已開始下跌，不過管理層認為運價可維持高檔
震盪，接下來還有中國農曆年前小旺季，預期運價下跌幅度不會超
過 30%。 
3. 2027 年海運影響因素主要為 1.)紅海復航(目前聯盟航商已復航
70%航次，若全數復航將釋出約 5~6%運力)、2.)新船下水量大增
（預計超過 300 萬 TEU），公司預期 2027 年運價不會像 2H26 這
麼好，但對 2027 年海運並不悲觀。 
結論 
2026 年台驊營運成長主要來自海運價格提升，雖 2027 年海運價格展
望不如 2026 年，不過今年航商獲利豐沛，配發之現金股利仍可挹注高
額業外收益。以今日收盤價 71.00 元試算，股價約當 3Q26 每股淨值
77.6 元之 0.91 倍。

公司營運概況 
 台驊國際控股(2636 TT ，T3EX)為國際物流承攬集團，業務涵蓋海
運、空運、中歐鐵路、內貿物流、倉儲與報關；公司以「核心物流
平台、策略性產業投資、資本配置、資金管理」四大價值主軸分別
衡量營運表現。 
 股權架構：直接持股台驊物流(THI，海運/鐵路/內貿物流)、台灣空
運 (TEC ，空運/ 報關/ 陸運) 、 THI Japan 及 Skymaster( 韓國) 各
100%；間接持股包括 PDQ(北美，台驊物流持股 100%)、ATP(新
加坡高科技空運與保稅倉，台灣空運持股 88%)、Shipgo/世購國際
(跨境電商物流)、特易行通運(陸運配送)及樺嘉實業(報關)，後三者
由台灣空運持股 100%。 
 公司強調持有之海運公司營運穩定，今年海運公司獲利很好，明年
可收取現金股利值得期待，且公司的財務狀況和現金流是物流同業
中最好的。 
 空運，各家航空公司都有配合，國籍外籍都有，運價不一定國航比
較高，要看機型為主。今年不是運價的問題，不會壓低空運價格，
重點在優化服務。台驊空運主要客戶是近程線的供應鏈，正積極佈
局長程線的空運。

3Q26及前三季財務表現 
 1-9M26 營收結構：海運 124.45 億元(61.7%)、空運 51.41 億元

 1-9M26 地區別營收：大中華 122.94 億元(占 61.00%，年增

台灣 33.65 億元(占 16.70%，年減 8.27%，衰退因公司台灣區主

 3Q26 單季：營收 87.35 億元(3Q25 為 48.00 億元，年增
81.98%)、毛利 13.27 億元(年增 70.85%)、營業利益 4.35 億元(年

17.14%)、毛利率 14.98%(去年同期 16.07%)、營業費用 22.42 億
元(年增 11.88%，費用率 11.13%)、營業利益 7.77 億元(年增
35.52%，營益率 3.85%、年增 0.28ppt)、稅前淨利 12.80 億元(年
增 20.04%)、歸母淨利 10.36 億元(年增 30.32%)、EPS 7.57 元(去
年同期 5.81 元)。 
 1-9M26 營業利益 7.77 億元加計營業外淨額 5.03 億元，稅前淨利
12.80 億元；營業外主要科目包括利息收入+2.96 億元(資金管理)、
其他收入+2.56 億元(含股利收入)、其他利益及損失+0.84 億元、權
益法損益+0.09 億元，以及財務成本-1.41 億元。

產業概況 
 近期運價看法，預期運價到年底高檔震盪，接下來還有農曆年前小
旺季，預期運價不會跌超過 30%。 
 2026 年海運業獲利展望，2026 年航運三雄財報獲利會是歷史次
高，僅次於 2022 年。4Q26 獲利應該可以維持 3Q26 水準。 
 2027 海運展望，1H27 影響因素主要是紅海復航、新船下水量大
增，明年不悲觀，但不會像 3Q~4Q26 這麼好，尤其 9-10 月美線
運價幾乎來到疫情期間的價格。預期 2027 年運價美國線>歐洲
線，目前看美國通膨問題影響比較大，觀察點是 2Q27，公司認為
川普對外態度會反轉，包括停止軍援烏克蘭、加快美伊停戰等。 
 紅海復航，聯盟船大概 70%復航，約 30%還在繞行好望角（確保
若紅海封鎖，隨時可繞回好望角），若全數復航，運力可以釋放
5~6%。

### Page 3

台灣 台驊控股

十一月 13, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  3

 新船下水，預計 2027 全年有 300 萬 TEU 新船下水，船公司目前計
畫，若運價大幅下滑，新船下水時間可能會遞延一些。目前 20 年
以上老船佔比約 6%，如運價大跌，可能會拆船。 
 運力控制，航商現在很熟悉如何控制運力且現金流也穩健，手段包
括停航、空白航班、跳港、減班等，削價競爭狀況變少，公司認為
很難看到長期低運價。 
 航線運力調度，不是每個港口都可以靠超大船，且聯盟運力分配很
精密，運力調度沒想像這麼容易。 
 AI，幫助最大的是空運，運輸重視時效性，零組件和機櫃大部分都
是空運。

## Document 637: 对冲基金大幅低配科技股，高盛嗅到逼空机会：Anthropic上市日或成转折点

Source PDF: all report pdf/对冲基金大幅低配科技股，高盛嗅到逼空机会：Anthropic上市日或成转折点.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-19
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⾼盛交易台主管 Rich Privorotsky 认为，对冲基⾦科技股低配幅度达 11 个百分点，接近五年
极值，叠加市场波动趋稳，科技股反弹和空头回补的条件逐渐成熟。 Anthropic 创纪录 IPO
或成为资⾦回流催化剂，但美联储鹰派⽴场与经济放缓⻛险仍构成压⼒。
摘要：
对冲基⾦对科技股的极端低配，正在为市场反弹埋下伏笔，⽽ Anthropic 创纪录 IPO 的落地，可
能成为触发资⾦回流、推动空头回补的关键催化剂。
⾼盛交易台主管 Rich Privorotsky 在周四晨报中指出，对冲基⾦科技股净敞⼝较基准指数低配约
11 个百分点，接近五年来最低⽔平。与此同时，市场庄家的伽⻢敞⼝极度偏空，⽽波动率趋于
稳定，这些因素都可能为反弹创造条件。纳斯达克期货周四早盘上涨近 2% 。
这⼀潜在转折恰逢 Anthropic 登陆华尔街。该公司周四以每股 83 美元定价上市，⾼于此前发⾏区
间上限， IPO 融资约 813 亿美元，连同股东配售合计募资 1073 亿美元，刷新全球 IPO 纪录。创纪

录的认购需求显⽰，市场对头部 AI 公司的投资热情依然强劲。
不过，科技股反弹并不意味着市场⻛险消退。美联储鹰派⽴场与经济增⻓放缓预期相互交织，
市场正⾯临仓位修复与宏观压⼒之间的博弈。
对冲基⾦科技股低配接近五年极值，逼空条件逐渐成熟
⾼盛最新⼤宗经纪数据显⽰，对冲基⾦账⼾中的科技股净权重已降⾄约 18% ，⽽该板块在基准
指数中的权重约为 29% ，低配幅度达到 11 个百分点，接近过去五年的极端⽔平。相⽐之下，
2026 年 6 ⽉，机构投资者还曾短暂超配科技板块。
⾼盛指出，科技股基本⾯多空净敞⼝已降⾄近年来较低⽔平。过去两个⽉，机构投资者持续削
减 AI 相关仓位，将资⾦转向其他板块。
Privorotsky 认为，极端低配为逼空⾏情创造了条件。科技股此前已经经历⼤幅抛售，只要不再
继续下跌，就可能迫使投资者回补仓位。
技术⾯也提供了⽀撑：标普 500 指数勉强守在 200 ⽇均线上⽅，市场庄家的伽⻢敞⼝极度偏空，
但近期波动率趋于平稳。 Privorotsky 认为，市场稳定可能进⼀步强化稳定，吸引资⾦重新⼊

场。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
Anthropic 创纪录 IPO 提振 AI 热情，资⾦争夺成为市场焦点
市场交易员认为，本周⻩⾦、加密货币、铜以及英伟达股票遭遇集中抛售，部分原因在于机构
投资者为 Anthropic IPO 腾挪资⾦。科技股抛售与 Anthropic 创纪录融资，可能是同⼀轮流动性
调整的两个侧⾯。
Anthropic 最终以每股 83 美元定价，⾼于此前 70 ⾄ 78 美元的发⾏区间上限，发⾏规模从 8.25 亿股
扩⼤⾄ 9.8 亿股。 IPO 融资约 813 亿美元，连同股东配售合计达到 1073 亿美元，较 SpaceX 今年 6
⽉创下的 750 亿美元纪录⾼出约 43% 。
市场认购热情⾼涨。 Vanda Research 预计， Anthropic 上市⾸⽇零售投资者净买⼊规模约为 2.5
亿美元。 Hyperliquid 平台上的 IPO 前永续合约周⼆⼀度接近 99 美元，较发⾏价溢价约 19% 。此次
发⾏认购倍数达到 6 倍，零售投资者获配⽐例最⾼达 20% 。

不过， Privorotsky 也提醒投资者关注估值⻛险。按照 83 美元的发⾏价计算， Anthropic 对应估
值约为其 814 亿美元年化营收的 18 倍。与此同时，公司第三季度增速已经减半，并背负近 9000
亿美元的承诺⽀出，其中⼤部分⽤于算⼒采购。值得注意的是，持有 Anthropic 约 260 亿美元股票
的投资者中，也包括向其提供算⼒的供应商。
债券市场逆势⾛强，开始为经济减速定价
美联储政策信号近期明显偏鹰。美联储理事 Waller 重申加息 50 个基点的主张，会议纪要显⽰，
“ ⼏乎所有 ” 联邦公开市场委员会成员都倾向于进⼀步加息。然⽽，债券市场反⽽出现后端收益率
下⾏，收益率曲线呈现⽜市平坦化⾛势。
澳⼤利亚就业数据进⼀步强化经济减速信号。该国 10 ⽉就业⼈数减少 4.35 万，远低于市场预期
的增加 2.5 万，此前 9 ⽉也减少 3.93 万。失业率升⾄ 4.9% ，达到 2021 年以来最⾼⽔平。连续两个
⽉就业⼈数下降，推动市场重新评估澳⼤利亚经济前景，降息预期升温，两年期国债收益率随

之明显下跌。
Privorotsky 认为，全球债券市场正在提前反映增⻓放缓⻛险。资本开⽀预期下调、⼤宗商品价
格回落以及通胀压⼒减弱，正共同推动投资者重新评估利率路径。
他将当前环境与 2018 年末的周期减速相⽐较，认为两者都具有后期周期特征，但政策含义有所
不同。当前利率⾛势表明，市场已经消化了相当⼀部分紧缩预期。不过， 2018 年末纳斯达克 100
指数曾从⾼点回撤约 25% ，美股经历圣诞节前后的剧烈抛售后，美联储才最终转向。

11 ⽉ 19 ⽇，宁波柯⼒传感科技股份有限公司（柯⼒传感，603662.SH）宣布完成对知⾏机器⼈

⽹披露，产品负载覆盖 0.1 公⽄⾄ 150 公⽄，已为超过 100 家具⾝智能企业供货。今年 3 ⽉，知⾏

业送样。

仍占主要部分。
2026 年三季报披露，柯⼒传感前三季度营业收⼊ 13.19 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 21.99% ；归⺟净利润
2.33 亿元，同⽐下降 8.30% ；扣除⾮经常性损益后的净利润 2.11 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 23.03% 。

归⺟净利润变动主要受所持驰诚股份公允价值变动影响，属⾮经常性损益。前三季度投资活动
现⾦流净额为 -1.23 亿元，上年同期为 -2609.87 万元，该⼝径包含理财与资本开⽀。
五笔投资均为参股，被投企业的收⼊未纳⼊柯⼒传感合并报表，机器⼈业务收⼊⽬前未在财报
中单独列出，后续可关注柯⼒传感与客⼾的联合⽅案何时进⼊产线。
